## 25. Non-Forgeable Launcher Record Framing

Every launcher control and artifact line shares one serial stream with ring-3 stdout. `sys_write(fd=1, ...)` routes caller-controlled bytes straight to `serial_putchar` (`src/kernel/sched/syscall.c`), and the host runner accepts any line matching its patterns as authoritative -- so a test binary can print a well-formed `UTEST: === N passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped of N total ===` and the boot-completion poll will accept it, stop QEMU, and report success without ever seeing the launcher's real verdict. The same shared namespace lets a binary inject `[UTEST-XML]` / `[UTEST-JSON]` records that no budget or counter accounts for. Predates §22 (the summary line and the XML/JSON streams have shared the namespace since §4 and §7) and is not specific to the report dimension, which is why it is filed here rather than patched there.

> [!NOTE]
> The threat model is a BUGGY binary as much as a hostile one: these binaries are built from this repo's own tree, so the realistic failure is a test that prints something matching a launcher pattern by accident. That does not lower the severity of the artifact being unauthenticated, it just sets the bar at framing rather than cryptography.

- [x] Every launcher-owned record is framed with a per-boot nonce carried in the klog SUBSYSTEM field, so a record reads
      `UTEST-1a2b3c4d: <payload>` where ring 3 can only manage `UTEST:`. The tag rides the subsystem rather than the marker because every record is bounded by klog's 256-byte message field and the JSON summary already measured 242 of 255 usable bytes: a `const char *` subsystem costs the wire budget nothing, where an in-marker `<nonce>` would be paid for by every emitter and push the tightest records over. All emit sites already funnelled through one macro (`utest_record_log`), so framing is a single change point. The nonce is `csprng_u64()` mixed with `rdtsc_ns()`, folded to 32 bits and never 0 (the "not generated" sentinel)
- [x] The nonce leaves the kernel on SERIAL ONLY. The live disk log is openable from ring 3 and klog appends and flushes
      each message to it synchronously, so without this a binary could read the announcement back BEFORE the first test task ran and forge a correctly framed terminator. `klog_set_disk_alias()` (new) renders the frame tag as the plain `UTEST` on the disk sink, applied inside `klog_disk_append` rather than at its callers -- the sink has TWO (the live per-message flush and the batch ring drain), and a caller-side substitution silently covered only the first while `kernel.log` kept all 112 tags. The announcement body carries no nonce copy for the same reason. Records stay in the on-disk log under the plain tag, so post-mortem diagnosis is unaffected
- [x] The nonce is generated ONCE PER BOOT and never rewritten, because `test_usermode_run()` is documented safe to call
      repeatedly AND klog retains the subsystem as a POINTER -- rewriting the buffer for a second run would re-attribute every retained first-run entry, including the crash-region evidence the next boot reads back. `KLOG_SUBSYSTEM_MAX` (new, in `klog.h`) now pins the serialized `klog_crash_entry_t.subsystem` field, the snapshot copy, and a `_Static_assert` on the tag, so the bound is compile-enforced in both files instead of a literal 16 repeated in three places
- [x] Host-side, every parser LEARNS the frame from the announcement and refuses anything failing it: the boot-completion
      poll, the verdict-line loop, the TAP/XML/JSON extraction, the report cross-check and the overflow/budget/abort gates in `scripts/test.sh`, plus `scripts/utest-json-harvest.py` and `scripts/test-swtpm.sh` (which keyed on literal `UTEST:` and would have accepted a forged summary while ignoring every genuine framed failure verdict). Learned, never pattern-matched: a poll accepting any `UTEST-[0-9a-f]{8}:` accepts a GUESSED one, which the ring-3 fixture proved by ending a run early with `UTEST-deadbeef: [UTEST-FRAME-END]`. Fail-closed both ways -- unframed launcher-shaped records with no announcement fail the run rather than reading as "nothing ran"
- [x] `[UTEST-FRAME-END] run=N records=M` is the run's completion signal, replacing the summary line the poll waited on,
      and every host runner reconciles its own count of framed lines against `M + 1` per run. A binary that prints a plausible summary and then hangs cannot produce it, and a truncated stream cannot reconcile. Emitted on BOTH launcher exit paths including the empty-suite early return. Each run brackets itself, so the artifact assemblers publish the LAST COMPLETE run rather than merging a repeat call's records into a duplicate-record refusal. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §22 (item: "The legacy `=== N passed, N failed, N skipped of N total ===` line is byte-identical")
- [x] A first announcement WINS rather than the run being refused on a nonce conflict: the launcher announces before it
      creates any ring-3 task, so the first is provably the launcher's, and discarding the frame would hand any binary that prints one string the power to throw away every genuine result. The conflict is instead reported as a failure -- correct results, loud verdict
- [x] `user/test/test_forge.exe` is the ring-3 adversary, wired into the manifest and the default suite: it prints a fake
      legacy summary, a guessed-nonce summary, a fake `[UTEST-XML]` testcase, a fake `[UTEST-JSON]` record, a fake report line and a guessed-nonce terminator, then passes. The proof is that none of it reaches an artifact or a verdict. It deliberately does NOT forge an ANNOUNCEMENT (the host fails a run on that, so a default-suite fixture emitting one would turn every run red by design); that path, repeat-run segmentation, count reconciliation and the refusal cases are asserted host-side against synthetic logs in `scripts/test-tooling.sh`
- [x] Commit: `"test: non-forgeable framing for launcher records"`

**Test checkpoint:** A test binary that prints a well-formed fake summary line and then sleeps past the poll interval does NOT end the run early and does NOT produce a green verdict; a binary that prints a fake `[UTEST-XML] <testcase/>` record does not reach `build/test-results.xml`; the real launcher stream still parses end to end with TAP, XML and JSON all enabled. Verified 2026-07-29 with `user/test/test_forge.exe` live in the default suite under `bash scripts/test.sh QUIET=1 TAP=1 XML=1 JSON=1`: its six forged records reach serial, `forged-xml` is absent from `build/test-results.xml` and `forged-json` from `build/test-results.json`, the fake 99/98 counts appear in neither, the run stays green at 17 user-mode binaries, and the framed stream reconciles (112 framed lines against `records=111`). Nonce containment checked by extracting the ring-3-readable `X:\Logs\kernel.log` from the booted image: 0 occurrences of the nonce and 0 frame tags, with all 114 launcher records preserved under the plain tag. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test.sh SUITE=exec` | 4 new kernel suites in `test_usermode_launcher.c` (`TEST_CAT_EXEC`) pinning the tag's exact host-visible bytes, its fit inside `KLOG_SUBSYSTEM_MAX`, its refusal of a short buffer, and the nonce fold never yielding the 0 sentinel; 1 new suite in `test_klog.c` (`TEST_CAT_BOOT`) for the disk-sink alias passthrough; 8 host-parser gates in `scripts/test-tooling.sh` (619/619 green) covering unframed refusal, wrong-nonce refusal, first-announcement-wins, repeat-run segmentation, count reconciliation, the disk-sink source invariant, and fixture wiring. End-to-end forgery rejection runs under `bash scripts/test.sh TAP=1 XML=1 JSON=1`.

> **Accepted:** [Critical] `sys_write` (`src/kernel/sched/syscall.c:73`) casts the caller-supplied pointer straight to `const char *` and dereferences it in kernel context with no range check, so ring 3 can make the kernel emit arbitrary kernel memory to serial -- which replays this section's frame tag without learning it (reason: a pre-existing arbitrary-kernel-read primitive in the legacy syscall surface, security-sensitive and owned by kernel hardening, not by the test framework; fixing it here would be an unreviewed syscall-entry change made unattended) -> XREF: `02-kernel-core/TODO-10-kernel-security-hardening.md` §31 (item: "Validate the whole `[buf, buf + len)` range against the user address window with overflow-safe arithmetic")

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped: a per-boot nonce in the klog subsystem field frames every launcher-owned record, and every host runner learns it and refuses anything that fails the check -- ring-3 stdout can no longer produce a line any consumer accepts.
> - Integrates through the single existing emit macro, so all record kinds gained the frame at one change point; `[UTEST-FRAME-END]` replaces the summary as the completion signal and carries the record count each runner reconciles against.
> - Downstream: `klog` gained `KLOG_SUBSYSTEM_MAX` (which now also pins the panic evidence block) and a disk-sink alias so an authenticating tag never reaches a ring-3-readable log; review consolidated the framing rules into ONE parser, `scripts/utest-frame.py`, that all three host consumers call, because the hand copies had already drifted apart.
> - Canonical docs: [`docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md`](../../docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md) "Record framing", [`include/kernel/klog.h`](../../include/kernel/klog.h) (`klog_set_disk_alias` contract), [`scripts/utest-frame.py`](../../scripts/utest-frame.py) (the parse rules).
> - Scope boundary: framing, not cryptography -- the bar is a record ring 3 cannot reproduce, not an authenticated channel; the nonce is 32 bits because the threat is accidental collision by a buggy binary that cannot observe the value.
> - Known hole, filed not fixed: `sys_write` dereferences its caller-supplied pointer with no user-range check, so a binary can have the kernel replay the tag out of kernel memory without learning its bytes -- non-forgeability holds only against callers that cannot exercise that primitive. -> XREF: `02-kernel-core/TODO-10-kernel-security-hardening.md` §31 (item: "Validate the whole `[buf, buf + len)` range against the user address window with overflow-safe arithmetic")

> **Accepted:** [Critical] `sys_write` (`src/kernel/sched/syscall.c:73`) dereferences its caller-supplied pointer with no user-range check, so ring 3 can make the kernel emit arbitrary kernel memory to serial -- which replays this section's frame tag without learning it (reason: a pre-existing arbitrary-kernel-read primitive in the legacy syscall surface, security-sensitive and owned by kernel hardening, not by the test framework) -> XREF: `02-kernel-core/TODO-10-kernel-security-hardening.md` §31 (item: "Validate the whole `[buf, buf + len)` range against the user address window with overflow-safe arithmetic")

## 26. Generator Hardening: Preprocessor-Faithful Constant Extraction

`scripts/gen-user-abi.py` read kernel headers with a hand-written scanner: §23 added logical-line splicing, comment stripping with string/char-literal state, and conditional-nesting detection, and each of those landed because an adversarial round found a shape where the scanner and `clang-19 -E` disagreed. Three consecutive rounds on the same class is the finding. Every disagreement had the same shape and the same severity: the generator certifies a value, both generated headers agree with each other, `make check-abi` passes, the crt0 fingerprint matches -- and the running kernel uses a different number. The scanner was fail-closed, so the known shapes refused rather than lied, but "refuses the shapes we thought of" is a weaker contract than "reads what the compiler reads".

> [!NOTE]
> Not a defect report against §23. That section's scanner was correct for the header it read, and the tooling suite pinned 39 cases including four clang-differential ones. This section removed the CLASS, so a future edit to `task.h` cannot reopen it.

> [!NOTE]
> The design review changed the shape of the answer and is worth recording: reading through the preprocessor is NOT sufficient on its own. Neither `-dM` nor `-E` returns an evaluated value -- `-dM` prints the unexpanded body `(TASK_EXIT_REASON_BASE - 1)` and full `-E` expands it to the TEXT `((-1000) - 1)` -- so a generator that stops at the preprocessor is still doing C arithmetic in Python and can still certify a wrong number. Extraction therefore only PROPOSES a value and `_Static_assert` makes the compiler decide, which also means the proposal rule no longer has to be right about C, only checked.

- [x] Extraction runs through `clang -dD -E` over a TU including all four allowlisted headers, in the kernel's OWN flag
      vector: new `print-abi-cppflags` Makefile target is the single source for that vector (`run_preprocessor`, `kernel_cpp_flags`). `-dD` rather than `-dM` because it reports every define/undef EVENT in order, which `-dM` (final state only) cannot. Splicing, comments, conditionals and include order stop being this script's problem; `splice_logical_lines` + `strip_c_comments` are DELETED rather than left dormant
- [x] Extraction only PROPOSES; `certify_values` compiles a generated `_Static_assert` per emitted constant against the
      real headers, so a wrong proposal fails the build instead of being frozen into two agreeing headers plus the ABI hash. Design review caught that `-dM`/`-E` return unexpanded bodies (`(BASE - 1)`, not `-1001`), so a preprocessor-only rewrite would still have been Python doing C arithmetic. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §23 (item: "`scripts/gen-user-abi.py` resolves signed and expression-valued constants through a SEPARATE fail-closed resolver")
- [x] Certification sweeps all 12 build-flavor combinations (`KERNEL_TESTS` x `EXCEPT_TELEMETRY` x `BUILD_ALT_BOOT`),
      so a constant whose value depends on the flavor is REFUSED -- the committed generated output is shared by every flavor and has no single correct value to publish for it (a header PAIR when §26 shipped; one contract since §28). Threaded, keeping `check-abi` at ~0.6s against 2.2s serial
- [x] Restricted expression grammar, fixpoint resolution, `int32_t` bounds and absent-name refusal all kept on top of the
      preprocessor's answer; single-owner enforcement replaces the old duplicate refusal and covers the two shapes `-Werror=macro-redefined` accepts silently (identical redefinition, `#undef` + redefine)
- [x] `main()` -- not just the fixtures -- enforces every gate: ownership across ALL published families (adversarial found
      it wired only into the exit-status path), a hard refusal when any ABI-prefixed name is unreadable by the single-literal rule (a skip would drop it from the FNV fingerprint as well as the header), an allowlist-completeness check so a kernel-side rename fails instead of shortening the table, and a range guard so an oversized value cannot certify truncated while the header publishes the untruncated literal
- [x] Certification refuses vacuous input: an empty family set would compile trivially and report the ABI certified, so it
      is an error rather than a pass
- [x] The INVENTORY is cross-flavor too, not just the values (`cross_flavor_names`): round 2 found that the flavor sweep
      could only assert names the single default-flavor extraction had discovered, so a constant defined only under `KERNEL_TESTS=off` was absent from the candidates, the skip list, ownership, the header, the FNV hash AND all 12 certification units -- every one of which still compiled. A name whose PRESENCE depends on the flavor is now refused by name, listing the flavors it is missing from
- [x] `TEB_LAYOUT` / `KUSD_LAYOUT` offsets are compiler-certified with `__builtin_offsetof` in every flavor. They feed the
      ABI hash but were verified only by a regex over raw header text, which accepts an assertion inside a comment or an inactive `#if` arm and never notices a deleted one -- so a field could move while a stale textual assertion held the fingerprint constant and the runtime handshake still passed
- [x] Fixtures repointed and grown 39 -> 109 in `scripts/test-tooling.sh`: shapes the old scanner refused because IT could
      not read them are now AGREEMENT assertions against clang; `every_published_constant_is_compiler_certified` is the whole-table differential that would have caught all five §23 findings in one pass; and eight `main_*` fixtures drive the PRODUCTION entry point with injected faults, asserting exit 1, an explanatory diagnostic, and that neither destination header was replaced -- the review's point being that a fixture calling a helper directly stays green if the call is deleted from `main()`
- [x] `clang-19` absent is an explicit named refusal (`clang_binary`) naming `scripts/setup.sh` and the `ABI_CLANG`
      override -- never a silent skip, never a fallback to the deleted scanner. Documented in [docs/infrastructure/development-tooling.md](../../docs/infrastructure/development-tooling.md)
- [x] Commit: `"abi: read kernel constants through the real preprocessor"`

**Test checkpoint:** `make check-abi` still passes and the generated artifact regenerates BYTE-IDENTICAL (at the time of §26 this was a PAIR of headers; §28 collapsed them into the single `abi/generated/abi_contract.h`) with `TASK_EXIT_EXEC_IMAGE_DESTROYED (-1001)` (verified: `git diff` empty after a full regeneration, hash `0x448A9D27775243B3` unchanged); `every_published_constant_is_compiler_certified` asserts the generator's value equals what clang computes for every allowlisted constant; each of the five shapes §23 refused now either resolves to the value clang selects (conditional arm, `#if 0`/`#else`, `/*` inside a string literal, conditional behind a block comment) or still fails the build (spliced `//` swallowing an `#endif` leaves it undefined; duplicate definition). Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` (109 `gen_user_abi:` fixtures, up from 39; 698/698 suite total) -- host-side generator, no kernel TEST_CAT surface; end-to-end proof is `python3 scripts/gen-user-abi.py --check` (rc=0 clean, rc=1 on a hand-staled header).

> **Notes:**
>
> - Shipped: extraction via `clang -dD -E` in the kernel's own flag vector plus a `_Static_assert` pass making the compiler the authority on every published value and every hashed TEB/KUSD offset.
> - `KERNEL_TU_FLAGS` + `print-abi-config` make the Makefile the sole author of the flag vector, the compiler and the flavor matrix; the generator mirrors none of them.
> - Runs inside `make check-abi` / `scripts/build.sh`, which now require `clang-19`; 0.65s for the threaded 12-flavor sweep, and the generated output stays byte-identical (a PAIR of headers when §26 shipped; one artifact since §28).
> - Downstream: `splice_logical_lines` + `strip_c_comments` deleted, so no second implementation of C phases 2-3 survives to drift.
> - Inventory is swept per flavor and ownership is enforced for every published family, covering the redefinition shapes `-Werror=macro-redefined` accepts silently.
> - Canonical doc: [development-tooling.md "make check-abi requires a compiler"](../../docs/infrastructure/development-tooling.md#make-check-abi-requires-a-compiler).
> - Scope boundary: the header PAIR still has no single atomic commit point (detection, not prevention) -- that is §28, split out before implementation.

## 27. Artifact Consumer Completeness: Run Identity and the Record Name Bound

The machine artifacts are now assembled, validated, and honest about whether the run finished, but they still describe a run that nothing can place. `build/test-results.xml` and `build/test-results.json` carry no timestamp, no commit, and no environment identity, and they are written to fixed paths that each run overwrites. The same commit is deliberately tested on several platforms (WSL2 TCG, WSL2 KVM, the CI-parity tier, native Windows WHPX, VirtualBox, bare metal), and this repo's doctrine treats a failure on ONE configuration as the finding rather than noise -- but nothing inside either artifact says which platform produced it, so results from two of them cannot be told apart or trended separately. Separately, binary names are bounded only by `VFS_MAX_NAME` (256) while every record is bounded by klog's 256-byte message field, so a long enough name still degrades individual lines.

> [!NOTE]
> `scripts/test-smoke-matrix.sh` is NOT the multi-leg producer here: it runs `test-smoke.sh` (boot-to-userspace validation) across its four legs and never invokes `scripts/test.sh`, so it produces no test-results artifacts at all. The multi-platform runs that DO produce them are the separate per-platform `test.sh` invocations. Any leg vocabulary this section adopts has to be defined for `test.sh`, not borrowed from the smoke matrix.

> [!NOTE]
> Not a defect report against the artifact work. The gates are fail-closed and the assembled documents are correct for a single run on one leg; this section is about what a real dashboard needs next, which the artifacts cannot answer yet.

> [!NOTE]
> Split 2026-07-29: the transport-VOLUME half (per-binary output capture, the skip-record burst budget, and the incremental host parse) moved to §29, and the record NAME BOUND moved to §31 after the design review. What is left is host-side only -- `scripts/test.sh` and `scripts/utest-json-harvest.py` assemble both artifacts, so identity is computed and published where the run's own facts already live, with no kernel edit at all.

> [!NOTE]
> Identity is inseparable from PUBLICATION LIFECYCLE (design review 2026-07-29, [high]). Stale-artifact removal happens only inside Step 6 (`scripts/test.sh:834`, `:852`, `:1033`), but the build failure at `:115-118` exits before all three -- so a failed run leaves the previous run's green artifact at the canonical path, and CI that uploads artifacts regardless of exit code attributes it to this run. Identity that is computed but published only on the success path would document that lie rather than close it, so the lifecycle is part of this section: identity is derived before Step 1, both targets are invalidated up front, and every exit publishes an identity-bearing document -- refusal or result, never silence over an older success.

Artifacts are written to `build/test-results-<leg>.{xml,json}` with the canonical unsuffixed paths kept as an alias for the invocation that wrote them, so two legs of one commit no longer overwrite each other
- [/] Evaluate `utest-json-v1` against CTRF (Common Test Report Format) before the schema has consumers. What IS sourceable

The field-by-field comparison is still UNSOURCED: web access was blocked for a second consecutive attempt, this time with the root cause identified. Identity reaches all three synthetic XML error documents and every JSON refusal envelope with `summary: null` / `errors="1"` untouched

WHPX, VirtualBox and bare metal stay unclaimable

Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

## 28. Atomic Publication of the Generated Header Pair

`scripts/gen-user-abi.py` USED TO write TWO destinations -- `user/include/abi_numbers.h` (consumed by ring-3) and `include/kernel/abi_hash.h` (consumed by the kernel) -- with crt0 comparing one against the other at `SYS_ABI_HANDSHAKE`. Each file was replaced atomically from its own uniquely staged temp, but POSIX `rename` is atomic for ONE path: a crash or SIGKILL landing between the two `os.replace` calls left a mismatched pair on disk, and every ring-3 binary then aborted at the handshake. The answer was DETECTION rather than prevention -- `--check` compared both destinations and the build wrapper ran it before compilation, so a split pair failed the next build instead of reaching a kernel. Prevention needed a single commit point for the pair, which is a repo-wide layout question rather than a generator-internal fix, which is why this is its own section and not part of §26. SHIPPED: the pair collapsed into ONE generated artifact, `abi/generated/abi_contract.h`, published by a single `os.replace`; both former destinations are now static committed shims over it, so there is no pair left to desynchronize and no window to crash inside. The rejected alternative (an atomically swapped generated-include directory) and the full consequence list are recorded on the first checklist item.

> [!NOTE]
> Split out of §26 (2026-07-29) because the two halves have different blast radii: §26 changes how the generator READS kernel headers and touches one script plus its fixtures, while this section changes how generated headers are LAID OUT and INCLUDED across the kernel and ring-3 build. Bundling them would have put an ABI-surface layout change behind a scanner refactor's review.

- [x] Chose the commit-point mechanism and recorded the rejected alternative: ONE generated header both sides consume,
      published as `abi/generated/abi_contract.h` (`OUT_CONTRACT` in `scripts/gen-user-abi.py`).
      - **REJECTED the atomically swapped generated-include directory**: POSIX `rename` refuses a non-empty directory, so it degenerates to a symlink swap, and symlinks in a COMMITTED tree are hostile to the native-Windows and bare-metal paths this repo validates on.
      - **The "kernel then includes a header carrying ring-3 numbers" cost is paid by two static shims plus two INDEPENDENT include guards**: `include/kernel/abi_hash.h` includes the contract without `ABI_CONTRACT_WANT_NUMBERS` (fingerprint only, so no collision with the kernel's own `SYS_*`), `user/include/abi_numbers.h` defines it first.
      - **Include-path consequence: NONE** -- the committed ABI root is deliberately kept OFF every include vector and both shims spell the include `"../../abi/generated/abi_contract.h"`, relative to themselves.
      - **An `-I` search was implemented first and REMOVED during review**: `-I$(BUILD_DIR)` precedes any late addition and `build/generated/` already exists, so a planted `build/generated/abi_contract.h` won the lookup and compiled a bogus fingerprint into the kernel while `--check` (which reads the committed artifact by absolute path) stayed silent.
      - **Build-dependency consequence**: `$(ABI_CONTRACT_H)` listed on the grouped userland target; crt0 link unchanged.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §23 (item: "`TASK_EXIT_EXEC_IMAGE_DESTROYED` is generated into `user/include/abi_numbers.h` (SUPERSEDED by §28: generated into `abi/generated/abi_contract.h`, which that path now facades)")
- [x] Implemented the mechanism: publication is a single staged temp, `fsync`, ONE `os.replace`, then a parent-directory
      `fsync` so the swap survives power loss and not just a process kill. With one artifact there is no interval in which a reader can observe half a generation, so an interrupted generation cannot leave a mismatched pair visible to a build. Generation stays idempotent -- a re-run over unchanged inputs renders byte-identical content and `--check` stays silent.
- [x] Kept `--check` and the corruption-injection fixture as the belt to the new braces, and RETARGETED the fixture:
      it previously rewrote the hash literal inside `user/include/abi_numbers.h`, which is now a facade carrying no literal, so the substitution would have been a silent no-op asserting nothing. It now corrupts `abi/generated/abi_contract.h` and hard-fails if the literal is not found.
      - **`--check` additionally validates both facades**, because a facade is ordinary committed source that no rendering comparison would catch. `make check-abi` runs exactly ONE facade check -- `check_shim_form()` compares each file against `CANONICAL_SHIM_USER`/`CANONICAL_SHIM_KERNEL` in the generator (restorable with `--write-shims`), newline-normalized since a CRLF checkout is compiler-equivalent, but exact in every other byte -- and that is the whole build-time facade gate.
      - **The second layer is EVIDENCE, not a per-build gate**: `check_shim()`, which preprocesses a facade under its own AUTHORITATIVE vector (ring-3 via the new `make print-user-cflags`, the kernel once per build flavor) and compares the ABI macros it actually resolves, is retained as the semantic canary in the tooling suite. Once the facade matches canonical text and the contract matches the fresh rendering, the resolved macros are DETERMINED, so sweeping 13 vectors per build was 26 clang subprocesses whose answer was already decided.
      - **FOUR successive parsing designs were bypassed in review before the byte pin:**
        - a `#define `-prefix scan (missed `# define`/`#undef`)
        - the same under minimal flags (missed `__OPTIMIZE__`- and flavor-guarded values)
        - per-vector value comparison alone (missed a guard on `NULL`, which `user/include/syscall.h` supplies by including `types.h` first)
        - a directive allowlist over stripped comments (missed line splicing, then `// /*` hiding directives from a block-first regex, then `/*` inside an `#include` header-name collapsing to the required spelling)
      - **Each was Python approximating C lexing and lost a different corner**; the facades carry no ABI data and never vary, so pinning their exact bytes is both the simplest check and the only one with no corners left to find.
- [x] Added a crash-window fixture that terminates DETERMINISTICALLY immediately before and immediately after the single
      `os.replace` and asserts the destination holds exactly one complete generation (old on crash-before, new on crash-after), plus a guard that the publication block performs exactly ONE `os.replace`. A timed `SIGKILL` was rejected as the instrument: it is flaky and can miss the publication boundary indefinitely. The fixture drives the PRODUCTION publisher (`publish_atomically()`, shared by the contract, `--write-shims`, and the fixture) through hooks either side of the real `os.replace`, rather than a transcription of the protocol that could not have caught a reordered or misdirected replace. Staging residue is stated at its true strength: an EXCEPTION at either side unwinds its staging file, so nothing is left, and that is what is asserted; a signal is a different case the injection cannot reach, since no handler runs, and a `SIGKILL` before the commit point does leave an inert uniquely-named `.tmp` that nothing reads and that can never take the published name.
- [x] Commit: `"abi: publish the generated header pair at a single commit point"`

**Test checkpoint:** a generation interrupted immediately before or immediately after the single `os.replace` leaves `abi/generated/abi_contract.h` holding exactly one COMPLETE generation (the old file or the new file, never a blend); an interruption raised as an exception additionally unwinds its staging file, while a signal cannot and leaves an inert uniquely-named `.tmp` that can never take the published name; the published mode is preserved rather than reduced to `mkstemp`'s 0600 (and a new destination lands at `0644 & ~umask`); a re-run over unchanged inputs leaves the inode and mtime untouched; publication REFUSES a destination reached through a symlinked path component and replaces a symlink or FIFO destination rather than writing through it; a FIFO facade makes `--check` refuse promptly instead of blocking the build, and a directory facade is told to remove-or-move rather than pointed at a repair that cannot work; `make check-abi` passes against the published contract; a hand-corrupted contract is still caught by `--check`; `--check` refuses a facade whose text is not its canonical form (newline-normalized); and the number tables resolve under either include order. There is no "both destinations" clause left to assert: one artifact means one rename, which is the whole point of the section. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM (build-host only for the crash-window and shim fixtures).

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` -- expect `723/723 tooling tests passed`, including the `gen_user_abi:` gates this section adds or retargets. Host-side only: the publication protocol is build tooling with no kernel test surface, and its runtime consequence is covered by the crt0 `SYS_ABI_HANDSHAKE` path on every boot leg.

> **Notes:**
> - What shipped: `scripts/gen-user-abi.py` publishes ONE artifact, `abi/generated/abi_contract.h`, via a single `os.replace` plus a directory `fsync`; the two former destinations became static committed shims over it.
> - How it integrates: both shims include the contract by a path relative to themselves and the ABI root stays OFF every include vector; `$(ABI_CONTRACT_H)` joins the grouped userland prerequisites.
> - Downstream effects: the ABI hash is unchanged (`0x448A9D27775243B3`) -- only packaging moved; `--check` gained a canonical-text facade gate plus `make print-user-cflags`, and the per-vector semantic check moved to the tooling suite.
> - Canonical doc: the `HEADER_TEMPLATE` preamble in `scripts/gen-user-abi.py` carries the single-commit-point rationale; the `CANONICAL_SHIM_*` comment records the four bypassed parsing designs and why the canonical TEXT is pinned.
> - Scope boundary: owns the publication protocol and the layout it needs. Userland rebuilds unconditionally today (`sysroot` is `.PHONY`), so the explicit `$(ABI_CONTRACT_H)` edge is hardening, not a live-bug fix.
> **Verified:** 2026-07-29 | commit `cd1bf643` | 5/5 items | build OK | 27037 kernel + 17 user-mode PASS, 723/723 tooling, smoke matrix 4/4 (kvm/tcg x 1/2 cpu), lint 0 errors, `--check` rc=0
> **Accepted:** [H] `check-abi` gates only `all:`, so `make kernel`/`userland`/`system-disk` can build a bootable image against a stale contract while the fingerprint still agrees and `SYS_ABI_HANDSHAKE` succeeds (reason: predates §28 -- the two-header design had identical exposure; the canonical `scripts/build.sh` path runs `--check` before compiling and raw `make` is forbidden by CLAUDE.md; the fix is a non-phony stamp rewiring three top-level targets and needs its own regression). CLOSED 2026-07-30 by §32 -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §32 (item: "Wired it over the object LISTS rather than per recipe, order-only for objects and a real prerequisite for the grouped userland recipe" at line 1080)
> **Quality reviewed:** 2026-07-29 | Codex 3x (adversarial, consistency, perf) | 4M+2L fixed, 1H accepted-XREF | scope: kernel-code-quality

## 29. Serial-Transport Volume: Derived Burst Ceiling and Incremental Parse

Everything the launcher tells the host crosses one serial line, and nothing in the framework bounds how much of it a single run may produce or what the host pays to read it back. Two costs sit together here: `UTEST_SKIP_RECORD_BUDGET` (1024) was sized while klog's per-subsystem rate limiter was the real ceiling, and §24 moved artifact records to `klog_unrated` so the budget is now the ONLY bound on a burst; and `scripts/utest-frame.py` re-parses the capture from byte zero once per completion poll, materializes the selected run twice more for XML assembly, and holds the whole log in a shell variable beside it. Each is affordable on today's suite -- one skip block, 0.11s per parse pass -- so this is headroom analysis rather than a live failure; it is worth closing now because §33's per-binary output capture is the change that makes both of them live.

> [!NOTE]
> Split out of §27 (2026-07-29) because the two halves have different blast radii: §27 changes what the artifacts SAY, while this section changes how MUCH crosses the transport and how the host consumes it. Split AGAIN (2026-07-29) after the design review: the per-binary output-capture item moved to §33, because giving ring-3 stdout real provenance is a kernel `sys_write` framing change with fork-descendant ownership semantics, not the host-side parse-cost work this section owns. `serial_putchar` takes and releases the serial lock PER BYTE (`src/kernel/drivers/serial.c:82-88`) and `sys_write` loops it per character (`src/kernel/sched/syscall.c:88-102`), so a concurrent klog line splits a ring-3 write mid-line and no time-window delimiter can attribute the bytes between two markers -- that finding is what forced the split.

- [x] Re-derived `UTEST_SKIP_RECORD_BUDGET` as a transport-INDEPENDENT serialized-byte ceiling: 1024 became (`UTEST_SKIP_BURST_WIRE_MAX` 1 MiB - `UTEST_SKIP_REFUSAL_WIRE_MAX`) / (`UTEST_SKIP_RECORD_FANOUT` 6 x `UTEST_RECORD_WIRE_MAX` 334) = 517
      `UTEST_RECORD_WIRE_MAX` sums `UTEST_RECORD_LINE_MAX` with named klog wire costs read off the ACTUAL emission path of the two records it bounds: `u_emit_skip_records()`'s `[UTEST-SKIP-RECORD-BUDGET]`/`[UTEST-RECORD-OVERFLOW]` markers, both `LOG_ERROR` and both logged after `task_cleanup()` clears the launcher's color-scope flag (`test_usermode.c:2895` before `test_usermode.c:3062`).
      - **A post-ship review (2026-07-29) found the first derivation had modeled the WRONG klog branch**: it assumed the TEST-color path (one level-ANSI, one truecolor sequence, two resets) a record emitted with the color scope closed never takes -- the "UTEST-<8hex>" subsystem tag does not even match klog's explicit TEST-color check (`subsystem[5]` is `-`, not the required `\0`/`:`, `klog.c:1279-1280`).
      - **The real path is the plain `LOG_ERROR` branch**, `level_full_line[LOG_ERROR]` true (`klog.c:276`), which re-emits the level-ANSI sequence after the message and carries no truecolor sequence at all: timestamp, cpu tag, two level-ANSI sequences, badge, THREE resets (badge, post-message, and the unconditional trailing reset every record gets at `klog.c:1332-1336`), `KLOG_SUBSYSTEM_MAX`+2, truncation mark, CRLF.
      - **The prior value (346) happened to still be a safe upper bound by coincidence** -- it over-counted a truecolor sequence these records never carry by more than it under-counted the third reset -- so this was a derivation-accuracy fix, not a live safety gap.
      - **A second re-adversarial round (2026-07-29) found the corrected 331 STILL undercounted the timestamp field**: klog's digit loop buffers `sec` into a local `char tmp[8]`, so 8 digits (up to ~3.17 years uptime) is the true safe maximum the renderer can emit within its own defined behavior, not the 5 digits `KLOG_WIRE_TIMESTAMP_MAX` assumed -- widened 12 -> 15 bytes, landing `UTEST_RECORD_WIRE_MAX` at 334.
      - **The `tmp[8]` boundary itself** (a latent stack overflow past 8 digits) is out of this section's scope and filed as its own item (→ XREF: `02-kernel-core/TODO-04-system-logging.md` §14 "Serial timestamp render bound").
      - **The fanout is 6, not 3**: the adversarial review established that each of TAP/XML/JSON emits an overflow MARKER plus a fallback record at its `overflow:` label, so a name long enough to overflow every formatter costs six framed lines per skip and a 3x derivation would have understated the worst case by exactly 2x.
      - **Baud was REJECTED as the basis** -- serial init accepts five rates, preserves an unknown firmware divisor at SPCR baud code 0, and falls back to 38400 (`src/kernel/drivers/serial.c:57-69`), so a 115200-derived number is wrong by up to 12x.
      - **Three `_Static_assert`s pin it** (the original floor and worst-case-fits pair, plus a third added 2026-07-29 asserting `UTEST_SKIP_REFUSAL_WIRE_MAX < UTEST_SKIP_BURST_WIRE_MAX` directly so an unsigned-subtraction wraparound fails with the right diagnostic), placed after `UTEST_RECORD_LINE_MAX` because a macro body expands where it is USED; the floor (budget >= `TASK_UTEST_REPORT_SKIP_MAX`) is the load-bearing one and was verified to fire by temporarily setting the allowance to 256 KiB.
- [x] Made the host-side frame parse incremental and offset-based, and replaced the poll's iteration count with a monotonic deadline
      `scripts/utest-frame.py` is rebuilt around a `FrameParser` class that is the SINGLE FSM: `parse()` and `parse_file(path, state, retain_lines)` both drive it, so the full and resumed paths cannot drift.
      - **State is versioned** and bound to the log's device/inode, committed offset and a sha256 anchor over the bytes before it (taken from the SAME descriptor that was parsed, never a second open), and validated field by field on restore including the cross-field rule that a complete run reconciles.
      - **Reading is binary**: `split_complete_lines()` mirrors universal newlines and refuses to consume a trailing bare CR or an unterminated final line, and `MAX_PHYSICAL_LINE` stops ring-3 newline-free output from growing the assembler without bound.
      - **The decisive rule is that persisted state may never establish a POSITIVE result**: any resumed parse that would report a completed run re-derives it with a cold parse from byte zero, so a forged or stale state can make the poll say "not yet" but can never make it say "done".
      - **`scripts/test.sh` derives `POLL_DEADLINE` from `/proc/uptime`** (monotonic -- `date +%s` steps under NTP or host resume). Measured: 13x faster over a realistic 59-incomplete-plus-1-completion poll cycle, verdict byte-identical to a cold parse.
      - **A post-ship review (2026-07-29) found the deadline was checked only at the top of the loop**, before the grep and frame-parse -- both unbounded in wall time -- ran; an iteration starting a moment before `POLL_DEADLINE` could still finish materially after it. The loop now rechecks the deadline immediately after `sleep 1` and passes the REMAINING budget to `grep`/`utest_frame_poll` as a hard `timeout` ceiling on their own execution, so a single slow poll can no longer run past `TIMEOUT` either.
      - **A re-adversarial pass on that fix caught a false-green regression the fix itself introduced**: `FOUND` was set the instant the kernel-summary grep matched, before the frame check ran, so a deadline expiring in the gap between them broke out with `FOUND` still `1` and no user-mode completion ever verified -- fixed by resetting `FOUND=0` on that exit.
      - **Also fixed**: `_load_state()`'s new size cap bounds bytes but not JSON nesting depth, so a deeply-nested sub-cap state file raised an uncaught `RecursionError` instead of degrading to the same cold-reparse fallback every other malformed state takes; `RecursionError` is now caught alongside `OSError`/`ValueError`.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §25 (item: "Host-side, every parser LEARNS the frame from the announcement and refuses anything failing it")
- [x] Streamed the selected run into the XML assembler: both whole-log shell variables are gone, replaced by an `XML_SRC` file the parser writes via `--emit-run`
      `STRIPPED` (the entire ANSI-stripped capture) and `RUN_SLICE` (the selected run again) were the second and third materializations after the parser's own list. The summary and testcase `sed` extractions now read the file directly, with an ANSI-stripped whole-log fallback when no complete run exists. `--emit-run` writes through a `.tmp` plus `os.replace` so a mid-write failure cannot leave a partial slice that `scripts/test.sh` would treat as whole, and output paths that alias the input log or each other are refused before anything is written. Measured on a real run: a 12,717-byte slice against a 269,997-byte capture.
- [x] Differential-tested the incremental parser against a full reparse -- `scripts/utest-frame-difftest.py`, 69 checks, wired as `scripts/test-tooling.sh` sub-test 6f
      Equivalence is asserted on EVERY append prefix byte by byte against an independent oracle (Python's own universal-newline reader driving `parse()`), as two properties rather than one equality: the resumed parse selects exactly the run a full reparse selects, and it may never ACCEPT what a full reparse refuses. Also covered: truncation, recreation under a new nonce, split ANSI sequences, an unterminated terminator, sticky `bad_close` survival, `MAX_TRACKED_CONFLICTS` 64/65 and `MAX_TRACKED_RUNS` 256/257 across a resume, over-long lines, seven hostile state files, a replacement preserving the anchor suffix, forged states at offset zero against empty and frameless logs, and the CLI aliasing/atomicity cases.
- [x] Commit: `"test: derive the usermode record burst ceiling and parse the frame incrementally"`

**Test checkpoint:** a re-parse of an unchanged 25 MB capture reads only the appended bytes and returns the same selection a full reparse returns, and never accepts what a full reparse refuses; a log truncated or recreated between polls restarts from byte zero rather than resuming past the new announcement; a forged state file can delay the poll but can never report a completed run; no shell variable ever holds the whole capture; the advertised `TIMEOUT` bounds wall time rather than a count of `sleep` calls. A ceiling-sized skip burst is bounded in BYTES (about 1 MiB) and deliberately NOT in time -- no supported UART carries that inside the default 60s deadline, the kernel cannot know the wire rate, and a run reaching the ceiling is the abuse the budget exists to bound, so the host deadline failing it is the intended outcome. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` (sub-tests 6f + 6g) | 728/728 PASS, of which `scripts/utest-frame-difftest.py` contributes 69 differential checks and 6g contributes 4 `utest_frame_poll` error-handling regressions (errexit-safety, killed-child, truncated-output, well-formed-passthrough); `bash scripts/test.sh XML=1 JSON=1 TAP=1` exercises the poll and run-slice assembly end to end, and a plain `bash scripts/test.sh` run confirmed the fixed poll loop against a real QEMU boot (27037 kernel + 17 user-mode tests passed).

> **Notes:**
> - What shipped: a derived transport ceiling (`UTEST_SKIP_RECORD_BUDGET` 1024 -> 517, from a 1 MiB byte allowance over a 6x overflow fanout) plus a resumable `scripts/utest-frame.py` and a monotonic poll deadline.
> - How it integrates: one `FrameParser` FSM serves the full and resumed paths, so the three host consumers cannot drift; the XML assembler reads a `--emit-run` file instead of two whole-log shell variables.
> - Downstream effects: 13x faster over a realistic poll cycle, a 12,717-byte run slice against a 269,997-byte capture, and `TIMEOUT` is now a real bound rather than a count of `sleep` calls.
> - Canonical doc: the `scripts/utest-frame.py` module docstring states the resume invariants; the derivation rationale sits in the `UTEST_SKIP_RECORD_BUDGET` comment in `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c`.
> - Scope boundary: owns transport VOLUME and host parse cost. Per-binary output capture moved to §33, which needs kernel-side stdout provenance no host delimiter can supply.
> - Security posture: persisted state may never establish a POSITIVE result -- a resumed completion is re-derived by a cold parse, so a forged state can delay the poll but never declare a run complete.

> **Accepted:** [L] klog's serial-line timestamp digit loop writes into a fixed `tmp[8]` with no bound on digit count -- a boot past ~3.17 years continuous uptime overflows it (reason: core klog.c rendering, not usermode test framework; §29 only needed to bound the wire cost WITHIN klog's defined behavior) -> XREF: 02-kernel-core/TODO-04-system-logging.md §14 (item: "Bound the timestamp digit loop in klog.c's serial renderer so it cannot write past its fixed stack buffer regardless of uptime seconds" at line 444)

## 30. Per-Run Artifact Records and Per-Leg Aliases

§27 made every artifact self-identifying and gave publication a fail-closed lifecycle, but the RECORD is still a reusable per-leg pathname. `build/test-results-<leg>.{xml,json}` is rewritten by the next run of the same configuration, and only the canonical pair is cleared at the top of Step 0a -- so an invocation that exits during the environment preflight, before its leg is even derivable, leaves an EARLIER run's leg artifact in place under the stable name. Identity blunts this rather than closing it: the stale document carries its own timestamp and commit, so a consumer that READS them can tell it is old, but one that merely enumerates `build/test-results-*.xml` cannot.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed from the §27 adversarial review (2026-07-29, [medium] x2). The recommendation was to stop using a reusable leg pathname as the primary record: publish each invocation under a unique run identity and update a per-leg alias only once the record is complete. That also subsumes the concurrency finding -- `scripts/test.sh` already serializes by construction (it patches the shared `boot.conf` and writes a fixed `build/test.log` and OVMF vars copy, so two same-tree runs corrupt each other's boot state long before their artifacts collide), and a per-run record is what would make concurrent runs meaningful rather than merely non-colliding.

- [x] Published each invocation under a unique run record and reduced both stable pathnames to aliases -- `build/test-runs/<run_id>/` is the record, written before the aliases
      `RUN_ID` (compact UTC timestamp, pid, `/dev/urandom` suffix) is derived in Step 0a ahead of EVERY exit path, and the directory is created with a non-recursive `mkdir` that fails if it exists, so the identity is collision-proof rather than merely improbable.
      - **Publication is settle -> commit marker -> aliases, each step gating the next**: `utest_finalize_record()` first gives every format the run OWED but never published its refusal document, then writes the marker, and publishes NO alias at all if the marker cannot land.
      - **The canonical pair goes through `utest_alias_record()` per format**; the leg pair goes through `utest_publish_leg_set()`, which stages every carried document before touching a stable name, clears the whole old set with a CHECKED removal, and only then moves the staged files into place.
      - **Five re-adversarial rounds drove that shape**: aliases published before the marker exposed an uncommitted record; replacing the leg pair one file at a time paired the new document with the other format's previous generation; and an unchecked clear could publish beside a survivor.
      - **Finalization runs before the final verdict** so an alias failure still fails the run. The preflight-exit hole is closed at the ONLY name that claims to be current: the canonical pair is cleared in Step 0a before anything can fail. `run_id` rides in both artifact formats.
- [x] Gave each invocation private staging inside its own record directory
      The refusal documents, the `.staged` siblings, the `$canonical.tmp` alias temp, the identity file and the run slice were all fixed paths under `build/`. They now live in `build/test-runs/<run_id>/`, so no two invocations can share one, and a tooling regression asserts no staging file survives publication. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §27 (item: "Publish on a lifecycle that cannot outlive its run" at line 1010)
- [x] Decided: concurrent same-tree runs are NOT a supported mode, and the refusal is now EXPLICIT rather than emergent
      `scripts/test.sh` takes an exclusive `flock` on `build/.test-run.lock` and exits 1 when another run holds it. Supporting concurrency for real needs per-invocation `boot.conf`, `build/test.log` and OVMF vars paths -- a much larger change than the artifacts, and per-run records would make concurrent runs non-colliding without making them meaningful. Two orderings make the refusal safe: the lock is taken BEFORE the artifact lifecycle is armed, so a refusing run touches no shared name and publishes nothing; and QEMU is launched with the lock descriptor closed (`9>&-`), because `SIGKILL` bypasses the cleanup trap and an inherited descriptor would let an orphaned VM wedge every later run. A missing `flock` warns and proceeds rather than failing a lone run.
- [x] Gave §29's slice-failure refusal a PERMANENT regression that drives the PRODUCTION control flow, not just the emitter
      The design review's [medium] was that testing an extracted document emitter would prove the refusal RENDERS while saying nothing about whether an `--emit-run` failure still reaches it. So `scripts/test-tooling.sh` sub-test 6h extracts the helper functions AND the whole slice-failure REGION verbatim from `scripts/test.sh` and runs them against a fake parser that exits 1 -- automating exactly the injection §29 performed by hand. It asserts the non-zero verdict, `XML_PUBLISHED`, `XML_SUMMARY_OK=0`, an `errors=1` document at the record and BOTH aliases, that no testcase payload was copied out of the capture, and that the no-summary branch neither overwrote the precise diagnosis nor double-counted the failure. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §29 (item: "Stream the selected run into the XML assembler instead of through whole-log shell variables, so peak host memory stops scaling with the capture")
- [x] Bounded the durability: the newest `UTEST_RECORD_KEEP` (default 20) records survive, pruned at the START of a run
      Filed during implementation rather than planned: making every invocation leave a directory grows `build/` for as long as anyone runs tests. `utest_prune_records()` is a function precisely so the tooling suite can drive it verbatim, and it prunes at the start so an investigation's evidence is never removed by the run still writing it. The run in progress is never a prune candidate. The bound is normalised in BASE 10 and capped: `UTEST_RECORD_KEEP=08` passes a digits-only check and passes `-ge`, then aborts the whole run inside `$(( ))` where bash reads a leading zero as octal -- so the call site also sits AFTER the canonical clear and the trap arming, where an abort can no longer strand a previous run's documents under this run's names.
- [x] Commit: `"test: publish each usermode run under its own artifact record"`

**Test checkpoint:** a run that exits during the environment preflight leaves no artifact under any name a consumer would read as current, with an earlier run's record still retrievable under its own run identity; two sequential runs of the same leg both remain readable afterwards; a per-leg alias always resolves to a complete document. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` (sub-tests 6h + 6i + i12-i12f) | 757/757 PASS, of which 6h contributes 15 behavioral regressions driving the extracted production span and the real finalizer (slice-failure refusal reached and not overwritten, record + both alias kinds, slice path inside the record, no capture payload, commit marker, no surviving staging, retention, marker-failure publishes no alias, incomplete cannot replace a leg alias, complete does, idempotent re-finalize, identity published last, owed-format refusal before the marker, absent format dropped from the leg set, the old set gone before any new document is published, staging failure keeps the previous set and leaks no temp, a partial clear publishes nothing beside the survivor, and a failed publish counts exactly one failure while sparing the other format), 6i contributes 4 publication-ordering assertions, and i12-i12f pin the structural invariants; the end-to-end suite with `XML=1 JSON=1` exercises the whole lifecycle against a real QEMU boot.

> **Notes:**
> - What shipped: per-run artifact records under `build/test-runs/<run_id>/`, both stable pathnames reduced to aliases, private staging, an explicit concurrent-run refusal, and a production-path regression for §29's slice-failure refusal.
> - How it integrates: `utest_finalize_record()` settles owed refusals, writes `record-complete.json` (`utest-run-record-v1`), and only then publishes aliases; the leg pair is replaced as ONE generation by `utest_publish_leg_set()`.
> - Downstream effects: five duplicated inline refusal blocks collapsed into one `utest_xml_refusal_doc()` emitter, which is what made the refusal testable at all; `run_id` now rides in both artifact formats.
> - Canonical doc: [docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md](../../docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md) "Run identity and artifact paths" carries the record layout, both alias semantics and the concurrency decision.
> - Scope boundary: owns artifact IDENTITY and publication lifecycle. An orphaned QEMU still owning the shared `boot.conf` after a `SIGKILL` is a pre-existing hazard the lock neither creates nor closes.
> - Security posture: a refusing second run touches no shared name and publishes no document; no alias is ever newer than its record, and every failure resolves to ABSENT or all-previous, never a pair drawn from two runs.

> **Accepted:** [H] an orphaned QEMU still owning the shared `boot.conf`, `build/test.log` and OVMF vars copy after a `SIGKILL` bypasses the cleanup trap (reason: pre-existing hazard the run lock neither creates nor closes; §30 only shut the inherited-descriptor half via `9>&-`) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §34 (item: "Detect a live orphan before touching any shared boot state" at line 1209)
> **Accepted:** [H] a reader holding an open descriptor on a leg alias keeps reading the previous generation's bytes, which no rename-or-pointer scheme at the writer can revoke (reason: consumer-side contract, not a writer-side defect; the record directory plus its marker already IS the generation-coherent artifact) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §38 (item: "Give the per-leg alias set a generation pointer, so a concurrent reader cannot pair two runs' documents")

## 31. The Record Name Bound and the Refusal That Must Be Counted

Binary names are bounded only by `VFS_MAX_NAME` (256) while every launcher record is bounded by klog's 256-byte message field, so a long enough name degrades individual records: the per-binary verdict line loses its `: PASS` / `: FAIL` token off the end, which stops matching the host's verdict grep in `scripts/test.sh` and drops that binary from the fail-closed recount. Each emitter has its own overflow fallback (the XML testcase drops the name, the JSON record substitutes `"overflow"`, the TAP point emits `unrepresentable`), so today's answer to a too-long name is per-emitter degradation rather than one refusal at the gate. `u_is_valid_manifest_name` (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c:235`) is the single choke point both the manifest path (`:1026`) and the readdir path (`:2949`) already run through.

> [!NOTE]
> Split out of §27 (2026-07-29) after the design review turned this from a validator tweak into an accounting change. Adding a length bound to that validator ALONE would be a regression, not a fix: both call sites drop a rejected entry and continue (`:1026-1030` logs and continues, `:2949-2950` continues silently), so a rejected name never reaches `total_planned` (`:3035`, `:3060`) and a newly over-bound `test_*.exe` would vanish from both artifacts while the suite still reported success. Trading a truncated record for invisible non-coverage is the false-green class this whole area exists to close, and the fix touches the plan/run walk rather than the validator -- a different blast radius from §27's host-side identity work, which shares no file with it.

- [x] Derived the bound instead of picking one: `UTEST_MAX_BINARY_NAME` is the MINIMUM over every name-bearing record
      kind of `(UTEST_RECORD_LINE_MAX - 1 - fixed_k) / multiplicity_k`, each `fixed_k` summed from that kind's own format literals through `UTEST_LIT(s)`, digit widths pinned to each value's own cap (`UTEST_DIGITS_REPORT` from `TASK_UTEST_REPORT_MAX`) and `time_ms` CLAMPED at emit (`u_clamp_time_ms`) so its width is a fact rather than an assumption. Comes out at 37; the JSON skip_block (2N, 37) binds and the JSON binary record (39) is two bytes behind it, so "skip_block is obviously worst" would have been right only by luck. A per-kind `_Static_assert` plus a `>= 24` floor pin both directions
- [x] Restricted the accepted charset to `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` (`u_name_char_ok`), making both escapers the identity on every
      accepted byte, so the bound is provable rather than probabilistic. The set subsumes the old one-at-a-time path checks (`\`, `/`, `:`, control bytes) and additionally excludes `#` and space, closing the TAP-directive injection `u_format_tap_point` had to defend against separately
- [x] Separated "not a test file" from "test-shaped but refused" via `utest_name_verdict_t`: a readdir entry that is not
      `test_*.exe` is still ignored, while a refused entry is RETAINED by both enumerators (a dedicated `refused_names[]`/`refused_digest[]` array so refusals never compete with runnable manifest slots, plus `u_glob_next`'s out-verdict), counted into `total_planned`, and published by `u_emit_refusal` in a PREFLIGHT pass before any binary launches -- a FAIL testcase in XML, JSON, TAP and the verdict line the host recount greps, with `counters[1]` + `total_ran` + `rt.unreported` moving together so `not_run` reconciles and the harvester's reported+invalid+unreported partition still equals `summary.total`
- [x] Identified the refused binary without echoing untrusted bytes: `refused_<ordinal>_<sanitized prefix>_<8 hex>.exe`
      (`u_build_refusal_id`). Uniqueness within a run is the ORDINAL, by construction, because no digest width can guarantee it; the FNV-1a digest covers the entry's exact byte SPAN so it survives an embedded NUL and correlates the same bad name across runs. An embedded NUL is itself a counted refusal (`UTEST_NAME_REFUSE_NUL`), closing the bypass where `test_good.exe\0...` validated and launched as its harmless prefix
- [x] Made the completeness reconciliation run for COMPLETED runs, not only aborted ones: a planned binary that produced
      no result emits `[UTEST-RUN-INCOMPLETE]` and the host fails the run, so a refusal cannot vanish when the directory changes between the planning and execution walks -> XREF: §35 replaces the two walks with an immutable plan
- [x] Commit: `"test: bound the usermode binary name and count every refusal"`

**Test checkpoint:** a `test_*.exe` whose name exceeds the derived bound (and one carrying a character outside the accepted charset) is refused at ingest and appears in `build/test-results.xml`, `build/test-results.json` and the TAP stream as a named infrastructure FAILURE that fails the host run, with `not_run` still reconciling; a name at exactly the bound runs normally and its records carry the full name with no emitter falling back to its overflow path; a non-test file in `C:\` is still ignored silently. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `scripts\debug\kernel\run-exec-tests.bat` (SUITE=exec) | 11 new TEST_CAT_EXEC suites (bound-equals-minimum, at/over the bound, charset-vs-not-test-shaped, embedded-NUL span refusal, published FNV-1a digest vectors, refusal-identity bound + uniqueness, ordinal digit growth, worst-case record at the bound, taxonomy edges, filesystem-fold identity, time_ms clamp), 0 failures

> **Notes:**
> - What shipped: `UTEST_MAX_BINARY_NAME` derived per formatter (37, the min over 6 record kinds), the `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` charset gate, a refusal taxonomy, and `u_emit_refusal` publishing every refusal as a counted FAIL.
> - How it runs: refusals publish in a preflight pass before any binary launches, so a smoke abort cannot swallow them; `u_classify_name_span` is the choke point and takes the raw SPAN, so an embedded NUL refuses rather than truncates.
> - Downstream effects: the four tightest emitters became pure formatters so a bound-length name is formatted in a test; `time_ms` is clamped at emit; a COMPLETED run short of its plan emits `[UTEST-RUN-INCOMPLETE]` and fails the host run.
> - Canonical doc: [docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md](../../docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md) "Binary name bound and refusal records".
> - Scope boundary: owns the name gate and refusal accounting. The two-walk enumeration, the accepted-entry tail past `UTEST_MANIFEST_MAX`, and `utest_filter=` case semantics are owned by §35.
> - Security posture: only the sanitized identity reaches serial or an artifact, uniquified by a per-run ordinal rather than hash strength, and the charset closes TAP-directive and record-splitting injection at the gate.

> **Accepted:** [H] an ACCEPTED manifest entry past `UTEST_MANIFEST_MAX` is dropped with only a WARN, and the tail-runs-via-glob fallback cannot reconstruct a name whose binary is absent from the image nor its `type`/`expects_tasks` (reason: was pre-existing and out of scope for this section; CLOSED 2026-07-30 by §35, which deleted the cap and the fallback outright and routes manifest runnables straight into the enumeration plan) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §35 (item: "Stop losing ACCEPTED manifest entries" at line 1230)
> **Accepted:** [L] a refusal publishes as JUnit `<failure>` where this pipeline already uses `<error>` for the structurally identical suite-abort, so a dashboard keying on `errors=` will not see refusals (RESOLVED 2026-07-31 by §37: refusals and launch failures publish `<error>`/`status:"ERROR"`, the producer carries `errors=` as a subset of `failures=`, and both assemblers reconcile it against the records before projecting JUnit's disjoint columns) (reason: §31 reused the existing record kind so the host needed no new shape; changing it means extending the `errors=` accounting and the doc's element schema) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §37 (item: "Decided: a refusal belongs in JUnit `<error>`, and the accounting was extended to match")

## 32. ABI Validation on Every Artifact Target, Not Just `all`

`check-abi` WAS a prerequisite of `all:` only, so `kernel:`, `userland:` and `system-disk:` reached the compiler without it and `make system-disk` could build a BOOTABLE image against a stale contract: the kernel recompiles with a renumbered `SYS_*` while ring-3 recompiles against the un-regenerated contract, both carry the same stale fingerprint, and `SYS_ABI_HANDSHAKE` therefore SUCCEEDS while ring-3 calls the wrong handler. The canonical path was protected -- `scripts/build.sh` runs `--check` before compiling and CLAUDE.md forbids raw `make` -- so this was a policy-mitigated hole, not an open one, which is why it is its own section rather than a §28 fix: closing it changes the rebuild graph of three top-level targets and needs its own regression. Found by the §28 post-commit adversarial review; the exposure predated §28 (the two-header design had it identically). SHIPPED: `$(ABI_STAMP)` puts the check INSIDE the dependency graph, ahead of every object and the grouped userland recipe -- and the design review established that the old `all:` edge was never even ordered, since under `-j` a sibling prerequisite's recipe may run concurrently with the compilation it was meant to precede.

- [x] Added the non-phony `$(ABI_STAMP)` (`build/.abi-check.stamp`), whose recipe RUNS `--check` and whose CONTENT is the contract digest
      REJECTED deriving it from a written-out prerequisite list, which is what the plan first called for.
      - **The generator's read-set cannot be enumerated**: it reads six kernel headers (`syscall.h`, `service_numbers.h`, `ntstatus.h`, `task.h` plus `ob/teb.h` + `nt/kusd.h`, the last two feeding the FNV hash and both missing from this section's original "four kernel source headers" wording), the contract, both facades and itself, AND queries the flag vector back out of this Makefile through `print-abi-config` / `print-abi-cppflags` / `print-user-cflags` -- then preprocesses those headers with clang across a 12-cell flavor matrix, so its true read-set includes transitive includes no prerequisite list can enumerate.
      - **A list that goes stale is fail-OPEN**, which is the failure class this section exists to remove, so the check always runs instead (`.FORCE`, the house pattern of `$(KERNEL_TESTS_STAMP)` / `$(EXCEPT_TELEMETRY_STAMP)`). Cost is 0.835s, which the canonical `make all` path already paid through `check-abi`.
      - **Writing the contract's sha256 rather than a bare `touch`** keeps the mtime stable across runs, so an always-running check never churns a rebuild; a missing `sha256sum`/`shasum` fails the recipe instead of publishing an empty stamp.
      Post-ship review moved this recipe out of the Makefile into `scripts/abi-stamp-check.sh` and added a cheap cache key (every `*.h`/`*.inc` under the include roots, the resolved compiler's file digest AND a behavioral fingerprint under the real flag vectors, both flag vectors, the generator, the Makefile, and the check script itself) so `--check` runs only when that key moves: measured ~129ms vs ~835ms on a cache hit.
      - **EIGHT re-adversarial rounds against this fix** (round 8 the circuit-breaker cap the `review-todo-section` skill's own convergence doctrine sets for a re-adversarial loop -- NOT this repo's `review_round_guard.py` ceiling, which is a separate 30-round stall mechanism) found and closed, in order:
        - unlocked shared temp files letting a concurrent process publish another's unvalidated key
        - the key being blind to `ABI_CLANG` overrides
        - header concatenation ambiguous across a sorted-adjacent boundary
        - a wrapper-identity check that stopped at file bytes instead of a behavioral fingerprint
        - a root-completeness test and a dependency-closure test each hardcoded instead of reading the Makefile
        - a concurrency test that passed with the lock removed
        - a cleanup-trap ordering leak
        - a behavior probe using host-default macros instead of the real target
        - a lock test inferring "blocked" from elapsed time instead of a positive marker
        - a `*.inc`-blind glob
        - a systemic `set -e` pattern where standalone `VAR=$(cmd)` assignments silently aborted before their own diagnostic could run (found empirically, five call sites)
        - `xargs` running on zero matched files and producing a bogus non-empty digest
        - a hardcoded exclusion value
        - a symlinked contract/facade bypassing `gen-user-abi.py`'s own refusal by leaving the key unchanged
        - a symlinked directory being invisible to `find` without `-L`
        - a directory symlink NAMED `*.h` bypassing the directory-symlink refusal by name instead of dereferenced-type classification
      - **The mandatory consistency + perf dispatch after round 8 found four more:**
        - `sha256sum -L` is not a valid GNU coreutils option, so the leaf-symlink content digest ALWAYS fell through to an unverified `shasum` fallback (fixed: plain `sha256sum`, which already dereferences)
        - the shared-library `stat` probe spawned one process per library instead of one batched call
        - the dependency-closure test spawned a `dirname` subprocess per reported dependency instead of parameter expansion
        - the lock-path refusal is a check-then-open, not atomic
      - **The lock-path residual is accepted, not fixed**: `scripts/abi-stamp-check.sh` refuses a symlinked or non-regular lock path already sitting there before ever opening it (the verified, realistic case); a concurrent replacement racing the exact open is an accepted residual (bash has no O_NOFOLLOW open), same threat-model boundary as the shared-library-swap residual below.
      - Full incident history and rationale for each: the file's own header comment.
- [x] Wired it over the object LISTS rather than per recipe, order-only for objects and a real prerequisite for the grouped userland recipe
      One declaration -- `$(C_OBJS) $(LZ4_OBJ) $(LZ4_FULL_OBJ) $(ASM_OBJS) $(AP_TRAMPOLINE_OBJ): | $(ABI_STAMP)` -- because the design review found that FOURTEEN explicit object rules (`gfx_simd`, `gfx_simd_avx512`, `memops{,_avx512,_sse}`, `stb_truetype_impl`, `gfx_text`, `image`, `image_save`, `icon_store`, `cJSON`, `json`, `lz4_full`, `kernel/lz4`) bypass the generic `%.c` rule, so wiring the pattern rule plus the one lz4 override would have left every one of them ungated and gone stale again at the next per-file flag exception.
      - **A target-specific prerequisite adds the edge without touching a recipe**, so a new explicit rule for a file already in those lists inherits it.
      - **`$(LZ4_OBJ)`** (the merged `build/libs/lz4/lz4.o`, distinct from `$(LZ4_FULL_OBJ)`) was added to the declaration and its own sample after the re-adversarial round found the production edge had four independent terms but only two were asserted.
      - **Order-only for objects**: the stamp must RUN ahead of compilation but must never force a rebuild, and objects that consume ABI values already reach the contract through `abi_hash.h` under `-MMD`.
      - **`check-abi` was REMOVED from `all:` and declared `.PHONY`**: the stamp covers `all` from inside the graph and is ORDERED, which a sibling prerequisite never was under `-j`.
- [x] Regression fixture: `scripts/test-tooling.sh` proves the wiring structurally and the refusal behaviorally -- 23 sub-tests
      Structural half reads make's OWN expanded rule database (captured ONCE via `make -qp`, not `--print-data-base -n`, which still executes recursive sub-makes and let an earlier fixture read the `src/boot/uefi` sub-make's `all:` line instead of the root's) and asserts the order-only edge on an explicit-rule object, a pattern-rule one, `lz4.o` and `lz4_full.o`, the real edge on the grouped userland recipe, that `all:` no longer carries `check-abi`, and both `.PHONY` declarations.
      - **Behavioral half renumbers one `SYS_*` in a DISPOSABLE `git worktree`** (0.395s, 77 MB) and asserts `make kernel`, `make userland` and `make system-disk` each fail naming the stamp, with no kernel object and no artifact produced.
      - **Trap-restoring a tracked ABI header was REJECTED**: this suite already abandoned that pattern because no trap survives `SIGKILL` and an interrupted run really did corrupt the checkout. The worktree also makes the oracle EXACT -- it starts with no `build/`, so "no artifact" is ABSENCE, which a deterministic compiler rewriting byte-identical output could not satisfy.
      - **Direct-invocation additions** (bypassing `make`, isolating `scripts/abi-stamp-check.sh`'s own logic):
        - -I/-isystem root coverage read from the Makefile's actual `ABI_INCLUDE_ROOTS`
        - a 12-flavor `clang -M` dependency-closure proof that the generator's six headers never resolve under `build/` or the Makefile's actual `ABI_KEY_EXCLUDE` value

      `.FORCE` was never declared `.PHONY`, so it was an ordinary target name: an old repo-root FILE called `.FORCE` satisfies it and every `.FORCE`-driven recipe silently stops running.
