## 33. Per-Binary Output Capture at the Source

The artifacts carry only `reason` (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c:2587`, a 96-byte launcher-formatted string built entirely from kernel-observed signals), so triaging a failure from an artifact alone still means opening the raw serial log. JUnit has `<system-out>`/`<system-err>` for exactly this and neither artifact uses them. The obvious cheap fix -- bracket each binary with framed BEGIN/END markers and let the host slice the region between them -- does NOT work, and that is what this section exists to say: `serial_putchar` acquires and releases the serial lock PER BYTE (`src/kernel/drivers/serial.c:82-88`) while `sys_write` loops it per character (`src/kernel/sched/syscall.c:88-102`), so a concurrent klog line splits a ring-3 write mid-line; the launcher's own window deliberately spans sched/exec/elf klog output (`:2628-2632`); and a `fork()` descendant is a separate task the launcher never reaps. Time-window attribution therefore cannot prove which bytes came from the binary, and a per-task byte counter would legitimately disagree with the delimited region -- recording that mismatch as metadata hides corrupted attribution, while failing on it fails nondeterministically.

> [!NOTE]
> Split out of §29 (2026-07-29) by the design review, which rated the delimiter design [high] on exactly the provenance hole above. The replacement is source-level framing: the payload is wrapped where it is produced, under one serial-lock hold, instead of being inferred from position in the stream. It is a kernel `sys_write` change with task-ownership semantics, which is a different blast radius from §29's host-side parse-cost work -- and the two share no file. Ordering: §31 derives `UTEST_MAX_BINARY_NAME` and the counted-refusal path, so this section carries only a bounded sequence id in its records and maps that id to the binary through §31's accounting rather than embedding a name that cannot fit the 256-byte wire cap. Split AGAIN (2026-07-30) by the sequencer's SPLIT-RECOMMENDED complexity gate (7 open items spanning kernel, host artifact assembly, and a standalone doc/tool item): this section now carries only the kernel-side source framing and fork-descendant ownership, which is the one item with real SMP/lock-order blast radius. The artifact-emission and byte-reconciliation work that CONSUMES this section's record format moved to §36; the refusal-name correlator and the JUnit `<error>`-vs-`<failure>` classification decision, which depend only on §31 and share no file with the framing change, moved to §37.

- [x] Frame ring-3 stdout at the SOURCE: wrap each `sys_write` payload in an authenticated, length-declared record emitted under a single serial-lock hold, chunked to `UTEST_RECORD_LINE_MAX`, so the host never has to infer ownership from position
      Shipped for BOTH ring-3 write entry points -- the legacy `sys_write` (`src/kernel/sched/syscall.c`) and the SSDT `NtWriteFile` stdout branch (`src/kernel/nt/nt_syscall.c`), which had the identical unframed per-byte loop and would otherwise have let a binary bypass capture by using the other ABI.
      - **Each byte is read once** (`char c = str[i]`/`buf[i]`) and fed to the terminal echo, then to `test_usermode_capture_byte()`; that function escapes `\`, `[`, and everything outside printable ASCII (`< 0x20` or `>= 0x7F`) as `\xHH`. Escaping is not just the four originally-planned classes (`\`, LF, CR, `[`) because `scripts/utest-frame.py` decodes every line as UTF-8 with `errors="replace"`, which would otherwise silently and irreversibly mangle a raw non-ASCII byte before it could ever be reconstructed.
      - **Chunks are staged in a `struct utest_capture_ctx` LOCAL to each write() call** (`include/kernel/test/test_usermode.h`) -- never on `struct task` -- so accumulating bytes needs no lock even from a multi-threaded binary's concurrent writers; an earlier per-task-shared-buffer design needed (and an adversarial round then found buggy) a spinlock this redesign removes entirely.
      - **Chunks flush as `"[UTEST-CAPTURE] owner=<pid> seq=<n> len=<raw-bytes> final=<0|1> <escaped>"` records** through the existing `utest_record_log` macro, inheriting the §25 frame-nonce non-forgeability with no new authenticator; `seq` is an `atomic_fetch_add` on the OWNER task's slot, giving unique monotonic values (not a wire-order guarantee -- klog itself does not provide that across concurrent callers).
      - **A one-time `"[UTEST-CAPTURE-BEGIN] owner=<pid> name=<name>"` record binds the owner pid to the binary's name**; both the arm (`utest_capture_active`/`owner_pid`) and the BEGIN record are emitted from inside `task_create_internal()` (`task.c`) BEFORE the new task is published (`num_tasks++`), closing a publish-before-arm race.
      - **The mid-write flush check runs before staging a byte** (not after), so an exact-chunk-multiple write stays staged for the end-of-loop flush and correctly carries `final=1`.
      - **`test_usermode_capture_start`/`_byte`/`_end`/`_begin` are the `KERNEL_TESTS`-gated**, no-op-inline-in-release accessors, matching `test_usermode_color_active`'s pattern.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §25 (item: "Host-side, every parser LEARNS the frame from the announcement and refuses anything failing it")
- [x] Decide and enforce whether a `fork()` descendant's output belongs to the binary, then aggregate or exclude it consistently
      Decision: a descendant's output belongs to the same owner as its parent. `task_fork()` (`task.c`) inherits `utest_capture_active`/`utest_capture_owner_pid` unchanged via `TASK_UTEST_CAPTURE_INHERIT` (`task.h`) -- the opposite of the self-report reset the same constructor already does.
      - **`utest_capture_seq` lives ONLY in the OWNER's own task slot**: every task in a fork tree increments `tasks[owner_pid].utest_capture_seq` via `atomic_fetch_add`, giving an O(1) unique sequence number across the whole tree with no parentage walk, matching the "O(1) at write time" requirement.
      - **Known, accepted, out-of-scope limitations:**
        - `task_current()` resolves through the project's pre-existing GLOBAL (non-per-CPU) scheduler cursor (documented at `ssdt.c:36-41` for the identical `previous_mode` lookup), so under real multi-CPU AP scheduling a capture byte could in principle be attributed to the wrong owner pid
        - the `seq` value is unique and monotonic but does not additionally guarantee physical serial-wire order matches seq order across concurrent threads -- klog itself does not provide that guarantee for any of its callers (klog_emit releases its ring lock before the serial write)
      - **Both limitations need the same per-CPU current-task cursor work**; every other `task_current()`-dependent kernel behavior has the same exposure.
      -> XREF: `03-memory-concurrency/TODO-07-smp-phase2.md` §3 (per-CPU run queues)
- [x] Commit: `"test: capture per-binary output at the source, framed under the serial lock"`

**Test checkpoint:** a binary's stdout is captured byte-exactly under source framing even when interleaved with kernel klog output on the same serial line; a `fork()` descendant's output follows the documented ownership rule; an unescaped newline, non-ASCII byte, or record-shaped prefix inside a binary's own payload cannot forge a frame boundary or corrupt reconstruction. Verified 2026-07-30 with 10 new kernel-side unit tests (`test_usermode_launcher.c`, `TEST_CAT_EXEC`): pure-escape correctness (marker/control/non-ASCII classes, short-buffer refusal, empty input), the derived chunk-size bound, `capture_byte` declining an uncaptured ctx, the live wire record produced by `capture_start`/`_byte`/`_end` against the actual klog ring, the exact-chunk-boundary and chunk+1 regressions, and `fork()` inheritance on two zeroed scratch TCBs (no live boot infra). 27489 kernel + 17 user-mode tests pass; the existing default suite (13 spawned binaries) exercises the capture path on every run since every spawned binary is now captured by construction. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test.sh SUITE=exec` | 10 new kernel suites in `test_usermode_launcher.c` (`TEST_CAT_EXEC`), all passing; full default suite (13 binaries) green at 27489 kernel + 17 user-mode tests.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped: ring-3 stdout framed+escaped at the source in `sys_write`+`NtWriteFile`, chunked into `[UTEST-CAPTURE]`/`-BEGIN` records via the §25 frame-nonce mechanism; `fork()` inherits ownership via an owner-slot atomic seq counter.
> - Integrates through `test_usermode_capture_start`/`_byte`/`_end`/`_begin`: a per-write ctx LOCAL to the caller's stack, never on `struct task`, so no lock is needed under concurrent writers.
> - Downstream: `task_create_internal()` arms capture + emits the owner-binding record BEFORE task publication; `task_fork()` inherits via `TASK_UTEST_CAPTURE_INHERIT` -- both unit-testable without live scheduler infra.
> - Canonical docs: [`test_usermode.h`](../../include/kernel/test/test_usermode.h) (capture ctx + accessors), [`task.h`](../../include/kernel/sched/task.h) (ownership fields + macros).
> - Scope boundary: kernel-side framing + fork ownership only; artifacts move to §36, refusal correlator to §37; `task_current()` + klog wire-ordering limits accepted -> XREF: `TODO-07-smp-phase2.md` §3.

> **Verified:** 2026-07-30 | commit `7962ce85` + review fixes | 3/3 items | build OK | 27489 kernel + 17 user-mode tests pass | lint 0 errors
> **Accepted:** [M] `task_current()`'s global (non-per-CPU) scheduler cursor can misattribute a captured byte to the wrong owner under real multi-CPU scheduling (reason: pre-existing, project-wide gap already accepted identically for `ssdt_previous_mode()`; fixing it here alone would be inconsistent with every other exposed path) -> XREF: `03-memory-concurrency/TODO-07-smp-phase2.md` §3 (item: "Per-CPU current-thread cursor" at line 120)
> **Accepted:** [M] A force-killed task still mid-syscall on another CPU could emit a framed capture record after `[UTEST-FRAME-END]`, breaking the host's count reconciliation (reason: same root cause as the `task_current()` gap above -- proper per-CPU state is what would let the kill path definitively know a task has stopped running everywhere) -> XREF: `03-memory-concurrency/TODO-07-smp-phase2.md` §3 (item: "Per-CPU current-thread cursor" at line 120)
> **Deferred:** [H] `seq` is unique and monotonic but does not guarantee physical serial-wire order matches seq order across a multi-threaded binary's concurrent writers (reason: klog itself releases its ring lock before the serial write for ANY caller, a systemic property this section inherits rather than introduces; closing it needs either klog-wide ordering work or a §36 reassembler that sorts by seq) -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §36 (item: "Reassemble a binary's captured payload by sorting..." ) (RESOLVED 2026-07-31 by §36: `scripts/utest-capture.py` keys chunks on `seq` and emits them in sorted order, never in stream order, and refuses a gap, duplicate or unterminated tail rather than reordering silently.)
> **Quality reviewed:** 2026-07-30 | Codex 4x (adversarial, consistency, perf, re-adversarial) + kernel-quality-auditor + concurrency-evidence-mapper | 1H+4M+1L fixed, 3 open (2 accepted, 1 deferred) | scope: kernel-code-quality

## 34. Orphaned-QEMU Recovery

`scripts/test.sh` launches QEMU in the background (`:537`) and reaps it only from the `cleanup` trap (`:494-497`). `SIGKILL` bypasses that trap, so a killed wrapper leaves a live VM still holding the shared boot state the run owns: the patched `boot.conf`, the `build/test.log` serial sink, and the `build/OVMF_VARS_4M.fd` copy. The next run then patches `boot.conf` and truncates the serial log underneath a VM that is still writing to them, which is precisely the interleaving §30's run lock exists to refuse -- and the lock cannot see it, because the orphan is not holding the lock. §30 closed the half it could: QEMU is launched with the lock descriptor closed (`9>&-`), so an orphan can never WEDGE the lock either.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed from the §30 design review (2026-07-29, [high]), which named the two-sided failure: an inherited lock descriptor wedges every later run, while a close-on-exec one releases the lock with the orphan still owning the shared files. §30 took the second horn deliberately -- a wedged lock needs manual recovery, an orphan is detectable -- and this section owns the detection. The hazard PRE-DATES the lock: before §30 there was no lock at all and the same orphan corrupted the same files silently. Split (2026-07-30) by the sequencer's SPLIT-RECOMMENDED complexity gate: the per-leg generation pointer that arrived here from §30's re-adversarial round 2 is a CONSUMER-resolution contract over already-published documents, not a process-lifetime hazard, and it moved to §38 -- this section now owns only the QEMU process lifetime (detect, reap, attribute).

- [x] Detect a live orphan before touching any shared boot state, and refuse the run naming the pid rather than interleaving with it
      Shipped as `utest_orphan_guard()` (`scripts/test.sh`) plus [`scripts/qemu-orphan.py`](../../scripts/qemu-orphan.py), and it runs EARLIER than the item asked: immediately after the run lock, not merely before the `boot.conf` patch.
      - **The design review rated the original placement [high]** because below that point the script clears the canonical aliases, prunes older run records (potentially the record of the very run that leaked the VM), and REBUILDS `system-disk.img` -- an image the orphan holds open as a writable AHCI drive -- so a refusal at the patch would arrive after the disk was already rewritten underneath a live VM. `system-disk.img` is therefore a third owned path alongside `build/OVMF_VARS_4M.fd` and `build/test.log`.
      - **Ownership is proven by an EXACT `/proc/<pid>/fd` descriptor match** (no `lsof`/`fuser` dependency, and no `comm` gating -- `QEMU_BIN` is caller-supplied and need not contain "qemu"), with the ` (deleted)` suffix stripped because that is the MAIN case: `test.sh` deletes `build/test.log` before each launch, so a previous run's orphan holds a deleted serial sink.
      - **An UNREADABLE descriptor directory is indeterminate rather than absent** -- a non-dumpable process hides `/proc/<pid>/fd` from its own user, so skipping it silently let `detect` answer "clean" about a process it could not see inside [high]. The scan records those pids and the guard fails closed on exactly one of them: the pid this tree recorded launching. Scoping it there is the point -- refusing on every unreadable process would refuse most hosts, since any unrelated non-dumpable process would look like a possible holder.
      - **Default is refusal**; `UTEST_ORPHAN_REAP=1` reaps instead, for an unattended caller where a refusal halts every later gate -- but only a holder whose pid, `starttime` and `boot_id` match what this tree RECORDED launching.
      - **A descriptor proves a process is using one of our files, not that we started it**, and the adversarial review was right to call the original "reap every holder" loop [high]: a `tail -f build/test.log` or a disk-image inspector presents the same evidence a leaked VM does, so unattended reaping on that alone would SIGKILL an operator's unrelated process -- the shape of damage this guard exists to prevent, not to cause. Provenance ("is this OUR VM") and identity ("is this still the SAME process") are separate guarantees and both are now required.
      - **A detector that cannot run FAILS CLOSED** -- the adversarial review rated the original "warn and proceed" [high], and it was right that the missing-`flock` analogy does not hold: a missing `flock` is a static property of the host guarding a hazard the operator can see, while a detector that RAN and errored has produced no evidence either way about a hazard that is invisible by construction. Treating "no evidence" as "no orphan" is the corruption the guard exists to prevent, so the run refuses, the detector's stderr is surfaced rather than sent to `/dev/null`, and `UTEST_ORPHAN_UNCHECKED=1` is a SEPARATE opt-in from the reap one ("reap what you find" and "proceed having found nothing out" are different decisions).
      - **A refusing run touches no shared name and owes no document**: no record directory exists yet and no artifact trap is armed.
- [x] Make parent death deterministically reap the VM so the orphan window closes rather than merely being reported
      Authoritative mechanism on WSL2 (and every supported Linux host): `prctl(PR_SET_PDEATHSIG, SIGKILL)`, armed by [`scripts/pdeathsig.py`](../../scripts/pdeathsig.py), which then `execvp`s the real QEMU -- so `$!` is still QEMU's own pid and the existing kill/wait path, the poll's `kill -0`, and the closed lock descriptor (`9>&-`) all behave exactly as before.
      - **`SIGKILL` rather than `SIGTERM`** because the guarantee has to hold for a QEMU wedged in device emulation.
      - **The fork-then-prctl race is closed from the PARENT's side**: `test.sh` passes `--parent $$` and the helper refuses to `exec` at all unless `getppid()` equals it BOTH before and after the `prctl` -- a design-review [high], because re-reading `getppid()` afterwards proves nothing on its own (a parent that died before the FIRST read makes both reads agree on the already-reparented value, and python interpreter startup widens exactly that window).
      - **`--check` probes callability and refuses a set-id or file-capability target**, since Linux clears PDEATHSIG when credentials change across an `exec`; the resulting warning is printed from `test.sh` BEFORE the launch so it is not swallowed by the launch's own `2>/dev/null`.
      - **`setsid` + pidfile was rejected as the mechanism**: it reports an orphan, it does not reap one.
- [x] Record the QEMU pid in the run record so a later invocation can attribute and reap an orphan without guessing
      `record-complete.json` gains `qemu_pid` (a JSON number, or `null` for an exit that never reached the launch) and `qemu_state` (`none` / `running` / `reaped` / `unreaped`).
      - **`status` plus a pid was NOT sufficient**, per a design-review [medium]: the normal path finalizes BEFORE the verdict while the trap kills QEMU only at exit, so a `status: complete` marker could describe a run whose VM was still live. The single idempotent `utest_reap_qemu()` (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL, then confirm) is therefore called from `utest_finalize_record()` ahead of the marker as well as from `cleanup()`, making `qemu_state` a fact at the moment the marker claims it. Two review rounds shaped that function into what shipped.
      - **The escalation is ARMED BEFORE the wait, not sequenced after it**, because `wait` on a child is unbounded and a QEMU wedged in device emulation never processes SIGTERM -- written as "SIGTERM; wait; then SIGKILL" the SIGKILL is unreachable in exactly the case it exists for, and the run hangs forever holding the run lock with `boot.conf` still patched [high].
      - **The timer subshell carries `9>&-`** for the same reason the launch does: it would otherwise inherit the run-lock descriptor and, once cancelled, leave its orphaned `sleep` holding the lock for the rest of the grace -- measured live, the next run was refused with "another run of this script holds build/.test-run.lock" seconds after the previous one exited.
      - **The second entry short-circuits on `QEMU_STATE=reaped`** rather than re-deriving liveness from `kill -0`, since `QEMU_PID` deliberately outlives the first reap for the marker's sake while the number itself is free for reuse the moment the child is waited [high].
      - **The timer itself signals only under two conditions, and reaching them took three rounds.** Cancellation works by interrupting the delay, so a blanket "escalate even if the delay failed" turns every cancellation into an immediate kill of a just-released pid [high]; the delay's EXIT STATUS is therefore the discriminator, trustworthy precisely because the grace is validated first -- a positive number cannot make `sleep` fail on its argument, so a non-zero status means it was signalled.
      - **An earlier attempt used a cancellation file under `TMPDIR`**, which was withdrawn rather than hardened: a predictable path in a shared directory is a symlink-truncation surface and a denial-of-escalation path, and the exit-status rule needs no file at all.
      - **The second condition is a `starttime` re-check, captured SYNCHRONOUSLY in the parent before the SIGTERM** -- read inside the timer it is read after the signal and after a scheduling gap, so a VM that exits promptly can be reaped and its number reused before the read, leaving the recorded identity describing the successor [high].
      - **`QEMU_PID`/`QEMU_STATE` are initialised ahead of the first artifact trap** rather than at the launch, so no pre-launch exit path reaches a reap or a marker with them unset under `set -u`.
      - **The pid is ALSO written to `build/test-runs/<run_id>/qemu.pid`** at launch as `<pid> <starttime> <boot_id> <run_id>` -- the only copy that survives a `SIGKILL`, which by definition prevents any marker from being written -- plus the shared `build/.test-qemu.pid` the next run reads.
      - **That shared record is published by the CHILD, before it execs QEMU** (`pdeathsig.py --provenance`, written atomically and refusing the exec if it cannot be), because the parent cannot publish it in time: it only learns the pid after the fork, so its own write lands with the VM already running and a wrapper killed in that window left a VM this tree really did launch but could no longer PROVE it had -- unreapable under the provenance rule above.
      - **Neither file is authority to signal**: `reap` re-checks `starttime` + `boot_id` and re-confirms live descriptor ownership immediately before signalling, so a recycled pid is refused rather than killed.
      - **Re-checking is not sufficient on its own**, though, and the adversarial review named the gap [high]: every one of those proofs is a check on the pid, which is a NAME, and the descriptor scan between the last check and the signal is a full `/proc` walk -- easily long enough for the target to exit and the number to be reused. The signal therefore goes through a pidfd opened BEFORE any validation runs, so it either reaches the process it was pinned to or fails with `ESRCH`, and can never be redirected to a successor.
      - **Termination is then detected by pidfd READABILITY** rather than by signal 0, which a zombie still accepts long after it has released every descriptor it held. Hosts without pidfd (or with `select` unusable) fall back to the re-check-then-`os.kill` path, exercised on demand by `QEMU_ORPHAN_NO_PIDFD=1`.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §30 (item: "Published each invocation under a unique run record and reduced both stable pathnames to aliases")
- [x] Commit: `"test: detect and reap an orphaned QEMU before reusing the shared boot state"`

**Test checkpoint:** a run whose wrapper is `SIGKILL`ed leaves no QEMU holding this tree's OVMF vars copy or serial log, or the next run refuses with the offending pid named rather than patching `boot.conf` underneath it; a normal run is unaffected and pays no measurable startup cost. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM. Verified 2026-07-30 by experiment, not by inspection: `scripts/test.sh` was launched, its wrapper `SIGKILL`ed once QEMU was up, and the VM was gone 2s later (`RESULT: NO ORPHAN -- pid 2596585 died with its SIGKILLed parent`). With a real process holding `build/system-disk.img`, `test.sh` exited 1 naming `pid 2596986 (sleep) holds .../build/system-disk.img` and left `boot.conf` unpatched (`test=1` count 0). The stale pidfile that the killed run left behind was cleared by the next clean run, which passed. Startup cost of the clean-path scan is MEASURED at ~50 ms (dominated by python interpreter startup), not zero -- against a ~24 s suite that is ~0.2%. Suites green on both engines: 27489 kernel + 17 user-mode on KVM, 27465 + 17 under `CI_PARITY=1` forced TCG.

## 35. Immutable Enumeration Plan for the Two-Walk Launcher

`test_usermode_run` walks `C:\` twice: once to plan (`total_planned`, the TAP plan line, the task budget) and once to execute, with live children running in between. Any binary that appears, disappears or is renamed across that window makes the two walks disagree. §31 made the disagreement DIAGNOSABLE -- `[UTEST-RUN-INCOMPLETE]` fires and the host fails the run -- but the launcher still cannot say WHICH planned binary went missing, and a run that a directory change perturbs fails wholesale rather than reporting the one entry it lost.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-30 from §31's design review, which rated the two-walk window [high] against §31's "count every refusal" promise. §31 took the reviewer's stated minimum (reconcile and fail closed for completed runs, not only aborted ones); the immutable plan is the reviewer's preferred remedy and is a different blast radius -- it changes enumeration for every binary, not just refused ones, and needs storage for the glob-discovered names the manifest arena does not hold today.

- [x] Snapshot the enumeration once: `struct plan_entry` carries name, kind, refusal verdict/reason, digest, type and task
      cost, in a PMM-backed `struct plan_state` (`UTEST_PLAN_MAX` 256 entries plus a fixed-stride name arena, `UTEST_PLAN_PAGES`) rather than a second stack array. Name lifetimes are split deliberately: manifest names stay pointers into the manifest arena (stable, and a refused name may exceed the plan's stride precisely because over-length is a refusal reason), glob names are interned because dirent storage is reused by the next `readdir`. `_Static_assert(UTEST_MAX_BINARY_NAME + 1 <= UTEST_PLAN_NAME_SLOT)` ties the stride to the derived record bound so interning cannot truncate an accepted identity -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §31 (item: "Made the completeness reconciliation run for COMPLETED runs" at line 1176)
- [x] Execute the snapshot rather than re-walking: both execution phases iterate the plan and `readdir` is gone from the
      execution path entirely, so `total_ran` counts entries consumed from the SAME array `total_planned` measured. A binary removed between planning and execution is now published BY NAME as a counted `planned binary absent` failure (`u_binary_present` checks before launch) instead of vanishing from a second directory walk as an anonymous shortfall
- [x] Bound the snapshot explicitly and fail closed: `u_plan_alloc`/`u_plan_intern` set `overflowed` at capacity, and an
      overflowing plan publishes one `plan full` aggregate and runs NOTHING (`u_plan_drop_runs`) -- the same contract the refusal-identity overflow already took, because past the cap the plan is not the complete enumeration and cannot certify what it did not see. Plan-block allocation failure is a separate `plan alloc failed` aggregate published from static storage, so it cannot fall through to the zero-planned empty-suite success path
- [x] Stop losing ACCEPTED manifest entries: `UTEST_MANIFEST_MAX` is DELETED and the parser appends runnables straight into
      the plan, so the plan is the only capacity that bounds enumeration. The "tail runs via glob" fallback is gone with the cap that produced it (it could not reconstruct `type`, `expects_tasks` or ordering, and could not see a binary absent from the image at all); glob discovery now runs only when no usable manifest exists -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §31 (item: "Separated \"not a test file\" from \"test-shaped but refused\"" at line 1172)
- [x] Kept `[UTEST-RUN-INCOMPLETE]` as the host-side gate, unchanged, for entries that produced no result at all (the tail a
      smoke abort never reaches). A binary deleted between planning and execution is NAMED in both artifacts as a counted failure rather than left as an unrun slot: both formats reconcile record count against `summary.total` (`scripts/utest-json-harvest.py` checks `len(testcases) == summary.total`), so a reserved slot with no record makes the artifact unparseable instead of merely incomplete
- [x] Reconciled `utest_filter=` case semantics with the filesystem's: `test_usermode_glob_match` now folds ASCII case for
      both literal and wildcard forms via a shared `u_fold`, matching `ixfs_strcmp`. That unblocks folding the accepted-entry dedup, which §31 had to keep bytewise only because a folded dedup beside a literal filter could suppress a requested binary entirely. Filesystem identity does not settle manifest POLICY, so the dedup folds only metadata-identical entries; two case variants disagreeing on `type=`/`expects_tasks=` publish one `manifest duplicate conflict` failure and run under neither -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §31 (item: "Identified the refused binary without echoing untrusted bytes" at line 1174)
- [x] Commit: `"test: execute the usermode launcher from one immutable enumeration plan"`

**Test checkpoint:** a planned `test_*.exe` removed after the planning walk is NAMED in `build/test-results.xml` and `build/test-results.json` as a counted `planned binary absent` failure and the host fails the run; an unchanged directory produces the same artifacts as the pre-snapshot launcher. Shipped deviation from the drafted wording: `not_run` reconciles to 0 rather than 1 for that case, because the absent entry publishes a RECORD, and both artifact formats reconcile record count against `summary.total` (`utest-json-harvest.py` refuses `len(testcases) != summary.total`) -- a slot counted as unrun with no record would make the artifact unparseable rather than merely incomplete. `not_run` and `[UTEST-RUN-INCOMPLETE]` keep their §31 meaning: entries that produced no result at all. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test.sh SUITE=exec` | 17 new suites in `test_usermode_launcher.c` (`TEST_CAT_EXEC`): filter case-fold (literal + wildcard, non-matches still missing), identical-duplicate fold, conflicting-policy refusal on both `type=` and `expects_tasks=`, plan-overflow-runs-nothing, unbuildable-path rejection, manifest parse past the old 128-entry cap with metadata intact, terminal refusal, lossy-prefix non-suppression, refused-attribute veto, fully-filtered-manifest-stays-authoritative, plan-alloc-failure as a distinct state (via `pmm_alloc_fail_next`), name-arena rollback + interleaved compaction, over-length refusals not deduplicated, and five smoke-gate provenance suites (attribute-refused smoke aborts; a name-refused line never claims smoke policy; provenance survives a third declaration suppressed by an existing refusal; a deduplicated refusal keeps the provenance it absorbed; a filtered-out smoke refusal does not abort). All passing. **Note:** the fast-fail on an ABSENT planned binary is not unit-testable -- it lives inside `test_usermode_run`'s execution loop, which the test policy hard-bans from `test_*.c` (it takes over the scheduler and spawns user tasks); the derived smoke gate that feeds it IS covered above, and the execution path itself by the live `test=1` boot run.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped: enumeration happens ONCE into a PMM-backed `struct plan_state`; both execution phases iterate that plan and `readdir` is gone from the execution path, so `total_planned` and `total_ran` measure the same list by construction.
> - Integrates through `u_plan_init`/`_add_run`/`_add_refusal`/`_drop_runs`/`_free` in `test_usermode.c`; the manifest parser appends runnables directly, which is what let `UTEST_MANIFEST_MAX` and its "tail runs via glob" fallback be deleted outright.
> - Downstream: `utest_filter=` is now case-insensitive (user-visible, and only ever runs MORE of what was asked for); the accepted-entry dedup folds with it, refusing metadata-conflicting duplicates instead of guessing a policy.
> - Fail-closed set: plan-alloc failure, plan overflow, manifest refusal-identity overflow and an UNREADABLE manifest each publish an aggregate and execute NOTHING (an unparsed manifest may have declared `type=smoke`, and rebuilding from filenames would silently downgrade it); glob-refusal overflow publishes its aggregate and keeps running, because a dropped dirent refusal cannot be a duplicate of an accepted entry the way a forgotten manifest identity can. All fail the run.
> - Smoke gate is DERIVED, not flagged: each refusal record carries one `smoke_selected` bit (trusted `type=smoke` AND filter-selected, decided together so no merge can pair one line's type with another's selection), every collapse point OR-merges it, and one post-planning scan sets the abort. Replaced three per-site flag setters that review found four distinct routes past.
> - Canonical docs [`test_usermode.c`](../../src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c) (plan structs + name-lifetime rules at the `struct plan_entry` comment); scope boundary: the plan pins a NAME, not executable content, so a delete-then-recreate under the same name still runs the replacement -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §39.

> **Verified:** 2026-07-30 | commit `ad83b4b0` + review fixes | 7/7 items | build OK | 27546 kernel + 17 user-mode tests pass | smoke matrix 4/4 (KVM+TCG x 1+2 CPU) | lint 0 errors
> **Accepted:** [H] the plan pins a NAME, not executable content -- a child that deletes a planned binary and recreates the name runs the replacement under the planned identity, with every count reconciling (reason: needs content-identity digests, whose cost is a full extra read of every planned binary on the no-manifest path taken every boot, or VFS open-file pinning semantics IXFS does not define; a different mechanism and blast radius from enumeration) -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §39 (item: "Decide between content-digest identity and VFS file-object pinning" at line 1468)
> **Accepted:** [L] no mechanical net binds the `UTEST_RSN_*` literal set to the `UTEST_REASON_REFUSAL` size tree, so a future reason omitted from the tree would silently understate `UTEST_REASON_MAX` and narrow the derived name bound (RESOLVED 2026-07-31 by §37: `scripts/utest-reason-lint.py` enforces strict set equality in both directions as `scripts/lint.sh` Check 23, with comments stripped before membership and extraction failure exiting non-zero) (reason: pre-existing shape, and this section's four new reasons were verified present in the tree; the fix is a lint check, not a kernel change) -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §37 (item: "Bind the `UTEST_RSN_*` literal set to the `UTEST_REASON_REFUSAL` size tree mechanically")

## 36. Per-Binary Output Artifacts and Fail-Closed Byte Reconciliation

`build/test-results.xml` and its JSON counterpart carry no captured stdout at all today, only the launcher's own `reason` string -- and once §33 ships source-level framing, the host has a byte-exact per-binary payload with nowhere in either artifact to put it.

> [!NOTE]
> Split out of §33 (2026-07-30) by the sequencer's SPLIT-RECOMMENDED complexity gate. This section owns the host-side consumption of §33's record format: routing the captured payload into both artifacts and reconciling the byte count the record declares against the byte count the host actually captured. It ships strictly after §33 -- there is no record format to consume until the framing lands -- and shares no file with §37's refusal-tooling work.

- [x] Emit the captured text into both artifacts -- JUnit `<system-out>` and the JSON record -- bounded and escaped per artifact, with an explicit truncation field rather than a silent cut
      Shipped in `scripts/utest-capture.py` (`splice-xml`) plus `captured_output`/`captured_bytes`/`captured_truncated` fields in `utest-json-harvest.py`. The launcher's kernel-observed diagnostic reaches `<system-err>` from the `reason` string already on the `<failure>`/`<skipped>` element rather than from a second source of truth for the verdict.
      - **Bounding is separate from reconciliation and runs AFTER it**: the whole payload is validated, then the artifact copy is capped (64 KiB per binary, 1 MiB per run), so the artifact never disagrees with its own byte count the way a silent cut would. `aggregate_truncated` reports bytes actually DROPPED, not budget reached.
      - **Truncation is reported in three deliberately distinct namings**: the capture MODEL uses `total_bytes`/`retained_bytes`/`truncated`/`truncated_bytes`; the public JSON artifact uses `captured_bytes`/`captured_retained_bytes`/`captured_truncated`/`captured_truncated_bytes` per testcase; the JUnit XML keeps `<system-out>` TEXT-ONLY (byte-identical to `captured_output`) and carries a SUITE-level `capture.truncated` property of `<binary>:<retained>/<total>`, present only when something truncated. Attributes on `<system-out>` were rejected: the Jenkins/xunit `junit-10.xsd` models it as string-only, so a validating consumer would reject the whole report.
      - **The splice is STRUCTURAL** (`ElementTree` parse + re-serialize), not textual: the assembler keeps only physical lines beginning with `<testcase`, so a hand-spliced multi-line child would be dropped by the next run, and hand-escaping a payload containing `&` or `<` is a second way to emit an invalid artifact. CR is re-escaped rather than preserved because XML normalizes a literal CR to LF.
      - **Separate INPUT bounds refuse rather than degrade**: retention caps bound the artifact, not the process, so `MAX_RECORDS`/`MAX_ESCAPED_BYTES` bound what an abusive binary can make the host hold.
- [x] Reconcile captured-versus-declared bytes FAIL-CLOSED, now that both sides count exactly the same bytes
      A mismatch fails the run and publishes a refusal through the existing `utest_publish_xml_refusal` path (XML) and `_refuse()` (JSON), matching the stance both assemblers already take on an unparseable summary.
      - **One shared model, not two checks**: `utest-capture.py model` parses the canonical run slice ONCE and both renderers consume the result, because two independent reconciliations could disagree about whether a run is corrupt -- the one thing a fail-closed check must never do. The slice is built before either artifact step, so a `JSON=1` run without `XML=1` gets one too.
      - **`final=1` terminates one write() call, not the owner's lifetime** (`task.h`: "each call gets its own final=1 terminator, never coalesced with a later call"), so many final records are legal; the invariant is that the HIGHEST seq is final. `seq` must START at 0 (every owner constructor runs `TASK_UTEST_CAPTURE_RESET`) -- accepting the first OBSERVED seq as the base would let a lost leading record pass as complete.
      - **Silence must be provable, not inferred**: an authenticated capture-family line that does not parse is refused, because skipping it made a damaged record indistinguishable from a binary that wrote nothing. The decoder also accepts only the producer's exact wire shape (lowercase `\xHH`, and only for bytes the kernel escaper actually escapes), so a corrupted record cannot normalize into clean text.
      - **A captured binary with no testcase refuses BOTH artifacts** (`capture_population_drift`): both populations come from one run, so drift means a verdict went missing. The reverse stays legal -- infrastructure testcases, skip blocks and never-spawned binaries have no captured output.
- [x] Reassemble a binary's captured payload by sorting `[UTEST-CAPTURE]` records on their `seq` field, never on physical position in the serial/klog stream
      `seq` (an `atomic_fetch_add` on the owner task's slot, §33) is unique and monotonic but does NOT additionally guarantee physical wire order matches seq order for a multi-threaded binary's concurrent writers -- klog itself releases its ring lock before the serial write, so two concurrent callers' physical writes can land in either order under preemption (a systemic klog property, not unique to capture). Shipped: chunks are keyed by `seq` and emitted in sorted order, with a gap, duplicate or unterminated tail treated as corruption rather than reordered silently. -> XREF: `03-memory-concurrency/TODO-07-smp-phase2.md` §3 (item: "Per-CPU current-thread cursor" at line 120)
- [x] Commit: `"test: emit captured output to JUnit/JSON artifacts and reconcile bytes fail-closed"`

**Test checkpoint:** a failing binary's stdout reaches `build/test-results.xml` as `<system-out>` and its JSON counterpart with the launcher's diagnostics in `<system-err>`, bounded and escaped so a binary emitting a megabyte of noise or a forged record boundary can neither break the frame nor exceed the run's record budget; a captured-versus-declared byte mismatch fails the run and publishes a refusal artifact. Verified 2026-07-31 on the 17-binary default suite: 17/17 testcases carry `<system-out>`, the JSON record carries `captured_output` for all 17, the payloads are byte-identical between the two artifacts, and 14,195 bytes reconciled with zero mismatches. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` (group `Per-binary captured output (host reassembly)`) | 918/918 PASS incl. 42 new assertions; end-to-end `bash scripts/test.sh QUIET=1 XML=1 JSON=1` -> 27546 kernel + 17 user-mode PASS with both artifacts carrying identical captured payloads.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped: `scripts/utest-capture.py` (`model` + `splice-xml`), reassembling framed `[UTEST-CAPTURE]` records by `seq` into per-binary payloads with fail-closed byte reconciliation.
> - Integrates as ONE shared model: `test.sh` builds it from the canonical run slice before either artifact step, the XML side splices `<system-out>`/`<system-err>` structurally, and `utest-json-harvest.py --capture` attaches the same bytes.
> - Downstream: a reconciliation failure now fails the run and refuses BOTH artifacts; a JSON-only run gets captured output for the first time (the slice used to be built only inside the XML step).
> - Canonical doc: [`scripts/utest-capture.py`](../../scripts/utest-capture.py) module docstring (record grammar, invariants, exit codes).
> - Scope boundary: host-side consumption only; the producer-side per-owner/run-wide emission budget is §40, and `task_current()` misattribution stays §33's accepted limit.

> **Verified:** 2026-07-31 | commit `ca65c816` + review fixes | 4/4 items | build OK | 918/918 tooling, 27546 kernel + 17 user-mode, lint 0 errors
> **Accepted:** [H] A concurrent writer sharing an owner can mask an earlier UNTERMINATED write -- A emits `seq=0 final=0` then faults, B emits `seq=1 final=1`, and the sequence is contiguous with a final highest record, so the host certifies it (reason: the wire format carries no per-write identity, so NO host-side reconciler can distinguish A's missing tail from B's complete write; closing it is a producer/ABI change in the kernel emitter) -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §40 (item: "Carry a per-write identifier (or explicit write-start/end records) so every write() call is INDEPENDENTLY terminable, and reconcile each write rather than only the owner")
> **Accepted:** [M] A total capture-channel disappearance reads as success: an empty model is accepted because a testcase without captured output is legal for infrastructure rows, skip blocks and never-launched binaries (reason: distinguishing "spawned, capture expected" from "never spawned" needs a schema field the artifacts do not carry, and inferring it host-side would refuse legitimate launch-failure records and red every run) -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §40 (item: "Mark which testcases were SPAWNED (`capture_expected`) so a vanished capture channel cannot read as success")
> **Accepted:** [M] The host does not enforce the producer's `UTEST_CAPTURE_CHUNK_MAX`, so a chunk longer than the kernel can emit still reconciles (reason: the bound derives from three kernel macros, so a mirrored `46` in the host would silently refuse EVERY run the day that derivation changes -- a worse failure than the over-long chunk it rejects; the producer must publish the bound on the wire first) -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §40 (item: "Publish the producer's chunk bound on the wire so the host can enforce it without a mirrored constant")
> **Quality reviewed:** 2026-07-31 | Codex 11x (design, adversarial x3, test-coverage, consistency x2, perf x3, re-adversarial) | 10H+11M+1L fixed, 3 open (all accepted -> §40) | scope: kernel-code-quality (comment-only `task.h` contract fix; the rest is host Python + bash tooling)

## 37. Refusal-Name Correlator and JUnit Error/Failure Classification

`refused_<n>_<prefix>_<8hex>.exe` never echoes the raw name that triggered a refusal, so an operator staring at an artifact has an FNV-1a digest and no way to map it back to a candidate source file; and §31 shipped refusals as JUnit `<failure>` rather than `<error>` (the element convention for "never ran") purely to avoid extending the `errors=` accounting, a divergence that was accepted but never revisited.

> [!NOTE]
> Split out of §33 (2026-07-30) by the sequencer's SPLIT-RECOMMENDED complexity gate. Both items here depend only on §31's already-shipped refusal-identity and record-kind decisions, not on §33's kernel framing work. Filed as a host-tool-plus-doc-decision section; the classification item turned out to need the PRODUCER too, because JUnit's `errors=` has no honest host-only derivation -- so the shipped section changes `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c` as well and carries the QEMU legs.

- [x] Give an operator a path from a refusal identity back to the offending file. `refused_<n>_<prefix>_<8hex>.exe`
      Shipped as `scripts/utest-refusal-id.py` (`digest` + `match`), documented in [docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md](../../docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md) with the inputs to try per discovery path -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §31 (item: "Identified the refused binary without echoing untrusted bytes" at line 1174)
      - **Matching is on the WHOLE identity, never the digest alone**: a 32-bit FNV-1a is not collision-resistant (the kernel says so -- within a run, uniqueness comes from the ordinal), so a candidate must reproduce both the digest and the sanitized prefix, every match is reported, and two survivors exit `4` AMBIGUOUS rather than naming one.
      - **The input contract mirrors the two discovery paths byte for byte**: `--manifest` re-tokenizes a manifest the way `u_manifest_parse` does (`#` introduces a comment only after whitespace, attributes and the EOL are outside the span, an embedded NUL is INSIDE it), `--names-from`/`--nul-from` cover readdir basenames, and `--hex` is canonical for a name no argv string can carry.
      - **The two mirrored kernel rules are bound by golden vectors on BOTH sides**: the FNV-1a constants and the accepted charset are pinned by the same four digests the kernel unit test asserts, and `scripts/test-tooling.sh` fails if either side stops carrying them.
- [x] Decided: a refusal belongs in JUnit `<error>`, and the accounting was extended to match
      A binary that never ran is an `<error>` in both artifacts (`status:"ERROR"` in JSON), which is the convention this pipeline already used for the structurally identical suite-abort; the same now applies to a `task_create` that produced no task -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §31 (item: "Separated \"not a test file\" from \"test-shaped but refused\"" at line 1172)
      - **`errors` is an overlapping SUBSET of `failures` on the wire, and the HOST projects it into JUnit's disjoint columns** (`failures = failures - errors`): the producer keeps counting a refusal in both, so the legacy `=== N failed` summary, the serial fail-closed recount and the TAP stream keep describing one failure population and none of them changed.
      - **Reconciled before it is trusted, on both sides**: `scripts/test.sh` refuses the artifact when the producer's `errors=` disagrees with the number of `<error>` elements it harvests, when `errors` exceeds `failures`, or when the field is absent; `utest-json-harvest.py` makes the same three checks against the `ERROR` records.
      - **The overflow fallbacks preserve the classification** in both formatters -- a fallback that demoted an ERROR to a `<failure>` would refuse the run for a count mismatch it caused itself.
      - **The name bound is UNCHANGED at 37**: `"ERROR"` is one byte wider than `"PASS"`, and a first cut charged that wide status and the assertion-report triple to one record -- a combination no run can emit, since a never-ran binary submits no report. The JSON derivation now takes the maximum over the two REACHABLE shapes, and `u_format_json_testcase` drops the report fields for an ERROR so the precondition holds by construction rather than by call-site discipline.
- [x] Bind the `UTEST_RSN_*` literal set to the `UTEST_REASON_REFUSAL` size tree mechanically. Every reason string must appear
      Shipped as `scripts/utest-reason-lint.py`, wired into `scripts/lint.sh` as Check 23 (ERROR, opt-out `SKIP_LINT_UTEST_REASONS=1`) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §35 (item: "Reconciled `utest_filter=` case semantics" at line 1234)
      - **Strict set equality in BOTH directions**: a reason defined but unlisted (the failure that silently widens the derived name bound) and a tree leaf naming a macro that no longer exists are both reported, so a rename cannot leave half the pair broken silently.
      - **Comments are stripped before membership is decided** -- a reason mentioned only in prose beside the tree is not measured by it, and accepting that would make the lint agree with a build that is already wrong -- and an extraction that cannot find the tree exits `2` rather than reporting clean.
- [x] Commit: `"test: ship a refusal-name correlator and finalize JUnit error/failure classification"`

**Test checkpoint:** the correlator tool, given a candidate filename, reproduces the same FNV-1a digest the kernel embeds in a `refused_<n>_<prefix>_<8hex>.exe` identity for that name, and recovers a charset-refused manifest entry whose offending byte the prefix hid; refusals route to `<error>`/`status:"ERROR"` consistently across the XML and JSON assemblers, and a producer count disagreeing with the elements it describes refuses the artifact on both sides. Verified 2026-07-31 (post-review): 984/984 tooling assertions, 27593 kernel tests, smoke matrix 4/4, both artifacts published with `errors=0` on a refusal-free run and `binaries_reported=13` matching exactly 13 triple-carrying testcases. > - Reconciled before projected: both assemblers refuse a run whose `errors` count disagrees with the `<error>`/`ERROR` records it describes, exceeds `failures`, or is absent.
> - The review then met the two dimensions that had never met. `errors` must not exceed `binaries_unreported` (every never-ran path increments both), an `ERROR` record carrying the assertion triple is refused outright (`error_self_report`), and the ACCEPTED-report population is reconciled from both directions: the records carrying a complete triple must number `binaries_reported` and their sums must equal the `run_report` totals. Before that a stream could claim an accepted report no testcase carried, or pair one `ERROR` with `binaries_reported=1`, and pass every check.
> - The correlator no longer refuses the one identity shape it exists to cover: a manifest token whose FIRST byte is NUL yields an empty prefix, which the parser had called impossible. It is accepted and matched on the digest, flagged `NO prefix` so a digest-only hit is never read as prefix-confirmed, and constrained so only NUL-leading bytes can match it -- an empty prefix is a fact about the candidate, not a wildcard.
> - Downstream: refusals and `task_create` failures move to a dashboard's errors column; the derived name bound stays 37 because the JSON derivation now measures its two reachable shapes separately. The JSON binary record and the JSON skip_block are TIED at 37, not two bytes apart as the derivation's rationale claimed, and the completeness test now minimizes over all 7 record kinds rather than 6.

## 38. Per-Leg Generation Pointer for Coherent Document Resolution

A consumer of the per-leg artifacts resolves them by enumerating `build/test-results-<leg>.xml` and `build/test-results-<leg>.json` -- two independent paths that §30 can only publish in sequence, never atomically as a pair.

> [!NOTE]
> Split out of §34 (2026-07-30) by the sequencer's SPLIT-RECOMMENDED complexity gate; the item arrived in §34 from §30's re-adversarial round 2 (2026-07-30, [high]). It shares §34's ancestry in §30's shared-mutable-host-state class but not its subject: §34 owns the QEMU process lifetime, this section owns the contract a READER uses to resolve one run's document set. It ships independently of §34 -- neither blocks the other -- and touches the alias-publication code plus its doc, not the launch/reap path.

- [x] Gave the per-leg alias set a generation pointer, so a concurrent reader cannot pair two runs' documents
      `build/test-results-<leg>.run` (`utest-leg-pointer-v1`) is published by `utest_publish_leg_pointer` (`scripts/test.sh`) and names the record that leg resolves to; a consumer reads both documents out of that immutable record instead of enumerating the two alias paths.
      - **The pointer is NOT a member of the cleared alias set**, which the design review rated [high]: that set is removed and rewritten, so every member is briefly absent by construction, and a death in the window would leave the leg resolving to nothing permanently. One `mv -f` over a file that already names a complete, marker-committed record instead -- so the only observable states are the previous generation and the new one, both coherent.
      - **A torn or partially-published alias set does not advance it**, and a pointer that cannot be advanced is a counted `UTEST_FAIL` rather than a silent skew.
      - **Retention understands it**: `utest_prune_records` pins the records live pointers name (bounded by `UTEST_POINTER_PIN_MAX`, default 64, because `UTEST_LEG` can mint unboundedly many leg names) and sweeps any pointer left dangling, symlinked, or naming `.`/`..` via `utest_pointer_run_id`.
      - **34 host-only regressions** in `scripts/test-tooling.sh` extract the production functions verbatim, including the paused-reader checkpoint, two transition probes (pointer state at the first alias publish, and at its own rename), a bounded FIFO case, and discriminating bound tables for both retention knobs.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §30 (item: "Published each invocation under a unique run record and reduced both stable pathnames to aliases")
- [x] Commit: `"test: resolve per-leg artifacts through one atomically replaced generation pointer"`

**Test checkpoint:** a reader that resolves `build/test-results-<leg>.run` and then opens both documents from the record directory it names sees one run's XML and JSON or nothing, never a mixed pair, even when a second run publishes between the two opens; the documented resolution path is exercised by a regression that pauses a reader mid-resolution across an alias swap. Test on: host tooling only (no QEMU dependency).

**Test runner:** `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` | 1018/1018 PASS 2026-07-31 (984 baseline + 34 new: 22 `run record:` retention/containment/bounds, 12 `leg pointer:`); no QEMU or kernel dependency.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped `build/test-results-<leg>.run` (`utest-leg-pointer-v1`) and its emitter/publisher/validators in `scripts/test.sh`, plus pointer-aware retention in `utest_prune_records`.
> - Publishes as the LAST step of `utest_publish_leg_set`, only when the whole alias set landed; the two leg aliases are untouched, so every existing consumer is unaffected.
> - A pin is a BYTE-FOR-BYTE match against the emitter publication uses, so a pointer this script did not write cannot spend the bounded pin budget a valid one needs.
> - Downstream: `docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md` documents the pointer as THE resolution path, with a fail-closed algorithm that validates before resolving and never falls back to the aliases.
> - Scope boundary: the resolve-then-open window is closed by a bounded consumer-side re-resolve, not a lease (§45); the sweep's check-then-act pair is `timeout`-bounded, not atomic (§46).

## 39. Executable Identity for a Planned Entry

The enumeration plan §35 shipped freezes WHICH names a run will execute, and that closes the two-walk window: a binary that disappears is named rather than silently subtracted. It does not freeze WHAT will execute under a planned name. `u_run_one` reconstructs `C:\<name>` and the loader reopens that path (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c`), so a child that deletes a planned binary and creates a different file under the same case-folded name before the entry's turn gets its replacement launched, reported and attributed to the original entry -- with every count reconciling and the run green.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-30 from §35's design review, which rated this [high] against §35's own "immutable plan" claim. It is deliberately NOT §35's scope: closing it needs a mechanism §35 does not have, and both candidates carry costs that belong in their own section. Content identity means digesting every planned binary during the planning walk -- a full extra read of every test binary on the no-manifest path, which is the path every boot takes -- and comparing it against the bytes the loader actually reads before `task_exec`. File-object pinning means holding an open handle across execution, which needs IXFS unlink-vs-open semantics the VFS does not define today, plus a bound on simultaneously-open handles.

- [x] Chose CONTENT DIGEST over file-object pinning, and measured rather than estimated the cost that decided it
      - Measured on the live no-manifest path (the one every boot takes): `identity freeze: 17 binaries, 561302 bytes, 26ms (0 refused)`, matching the host-side `build/sysroot/test_*.exe` total byte-for-byte. Cost is ONE extra read pass, not the two the filing assumed -- verification runs over the buffer the loader already read, so that half is free
      - Pinning was rejected on a verified defect, not on cost: `vfs_rename_ex` (`src/kernel/fs/vfs.c:833-836`) gates `ref_count` only on the DESTINATION and only under `VFS_RENAME_REPLACE_EXISTING`, never on the source, so a pin is defeated by rename-away-then-create even though `vfs_unlink` (`vfs.c:749`) does refuse an open file
      - A design-review claim of mine was wrong and is corrected here: `vfs_open` defaults `share == 0` to share-ALL (`vfs.c:507-509`), so a pin would NOT have forced the loader open to weaken its share mode. The rename gap is the real disqualifier
      - SHA-256 (`include/kernel/crypto/sha256.h`), not the 32-bit FNV-1a beside it: `u_name_digest`'s own comment records that it does not claim collision resistance because the ORDINAL supplies uniqueness for a refused NAME. Nothing supplies uniqueness when the value alone decides whether adversary-influenced bytes may execute
- [x] Verified the identity over the exact bytes `task_exec` consumes, closing the window rather than narrowing it
      - The check lives in `utest_loader_func` between `vfs_read` and `task_exec` (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c`), NOT in the launcher before the spawn: `task_exec` takes a BUFFER, so hashing that buffer leaves no gap between what was verified and what runs. A launcher-side check would have left exactly the window this section exists to close
      - `u_identity_matches` compares all 32 bytes in constant time and treats a NULL expected identity as a MISMATCH, so "the launcher lost what was supposed to run" can never be the branch that lets bytes execute
      - A mismatch publishes the named, counted reason `UTEST_RSN_MISMATCH` ("planned binary replaced", 23 bytes, inside the pre-existing 29-byte `UTEST_RSN_SHAPE` maximum so the derived name budget is unchanged) through `u_run_one`'s existing reason/record-verdict path, in the same ERROR class a refused name gets -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §35 (item: "Execute the snapshot rather than re-walking" at line 1441)
      - Fail-closed at plan time too: `u_plan_freeze_identities` converts every entry it cannot digest into a counted REFUSAL before `u_plan_derive_smoke_gate` runs, preserving `smoke_selected`, so "a RUN entry carries a frozen identity" is structural rather than a flag anyone must remember to test
- [x] Added 4 `TEST_CAT_EXEC` regressions in `src/kernel/test/test_usermode_launcher.c` covering the mechanism over the real VFS
      - Identity is stable and content-bound: the same binary digests identically twice, an unresolvable name fails closed rather than returning a comparable zero digest, and rewriting a scratch file's bytes under an unchanged name changes its identity (plus a one-bit change in the last byte)
      - The verifier is fail-closed: a digest matches itself, and a NULL expected identity does not match
      - Sizes outside what the executor will load are refused BEFORE any read: a zero-length file cannot be an executable (and two empty entries would otherwise share one identity), and the ceiling is `EXEC_MAX_IMAGE_SIZE`, which also keeps the loader's `(size + 4095u)` page rounding from overflowing
      - An unfreezable entry becomes a counted REFUSAL rather than staying runnable, and an unfreezable SMOKE entry still trips the smoke gate
      - What these do NOT prove is the in-loader integration -- that the production `utest_loader_func` takes the mismatch branch during a real spawn. Raised by the design review and NOT closable by this run: the fixture needs a sacrificial planned binary staged onto the image, and staging one is a `Makefile` edit, which `receipt_surface_guard.py` BLOCKS for the unattended runner -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §47 (item: "Stage a sacrificial planned binary and prove the loader's mismatch branch fires end-to-end" at line 1802)
- [x] Commit: `"test: pin executable identity, not just the name, for a planned entry"`

**Test checkpoint:** with a fixture that replaces a planned `test_*.exe` with different contents after the planning walk, the run publishes a named identity-mismatch failure, does not execute the replacement, and the host fails the run; an unchanged directory produces the same artifacts as before the change. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Verified:** 2026-07-31 | commit `bdb9a21a` + this review commit (the review's own fixes are part of the verified candidate) | 4/4 items | build OK | 27611 kernel + 17 user-mode tests pass | lint 0 errors | identity freeze measured 17 binaries / 561302 bytes / 24ms (64KiB buffer)
> **Deferred:** [M] the loader's mismatch branch is proven only by helper tests, not end-to-end through a real spawn (reason: the fixture needs a sacrificial binary staged onto the image, which is a `Makefile` edit `receipt_surface_guard.py` blocks for the unattended runner) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §47 (item: "Stage a sacrificial planned binary and prove the loader's mismatch branch fires end-to-end" at line 1802)

> **Notes:**
> - The decision the section asked for was made on a VERIFIED DEFECT, not a cost estimate. File-object pinning loses to `vfs_rename_ex` (`src/kernel/fs/vfs.c:833-836`), which gates `ref_count` only on the DESTINATION and only under `VFS_RENAME_REPLACE_EXISTING` -- never on the source -- so a pin is defeated by rename-away-then-create even though `vfs_unlink` (`vfs.c:749`) does refuse an open file. A rationale of mine that pinning would force the loader to weaken its share mode was WRONG and the design review corrected it: `vfs_open` defaults `share == 0` to share-all (`vfs.c:507-509`).
> - Two [high] findings landed in review, both in the same shape -- a bit that was never set and a number that was already taken. The freeze conversion originally PRESERVED `smoke_selected`, but a fresh RUN entry carries 0 from `u_plan_alloc` regardless of type, so an unverifiable smoke prerequisite would not have tripped the gate; and the first regression hand-set that flag, so it passed against the defect. The mismatch branch originally exited `-6`, which is exactly `UTEST_EXIT_TIMEOUT`, and then `-7`, which is inside the `-(signum)` range -- resolved by allocating `TASK_EXIT_UTEST_IDENTITY` from the reserved block in `include/kernel/sched/task.h`, whose own comment says to add reasons there and never as a bare literal.
> - The perf finding's MECHANISM was confirmed and its MAGNITUDE was not: `ixfs_file_read` really does `kmalloc`/`kfree` a bounce buffer and stamp `i_atime` + `ixfs_write_inode` on every call, so 4 KiB slices cost ~147 allocations and ~147 inode writebacks per boot, and a 64 KiB buffer cuts that to ~18 reads. Measured effect was 26ms -> 24ms, so chunking was not the dominant cost. Kept for the I/O reduction, which scales with the suite; the wall-clock claim is not made.
> - Not stamped as a finding because it is a pre-existing file-wide convention rather than anything this section introduced: the freeze pass casts `pmm_alloc_contiguous` output straight to a kernel pointer and allocates on a path reached after `scheduler_enable`, exactly as `u_plan_init` and the loader's staging allocation already do. `pmm_alloc_pages_hhdm` has no production callers repo-wide, so changing it is a file-wide (arguably repo-wide) change.

## 40. Producer-Side Capture Emission Budget

§36 bounds the HOST: it caps what reaches an artifact (64 KiB per binary, 1 MiB per run) and refuses a run whose capture stream exceeds its input bounds. Nothing bounds the PRODUCER. A ring-3 binary can emit unlimited valid sub-256-byte `[UTEST-CAPTURE]` records, and every one of them costs serial wire time and log disk before any host code runs -- so a single chatty or hostile test binary can stretch a run's wall-clock or fill the log device while every individual record remains perfectly well-formed. The existing `UTEST_SKIP_RECORD_BUDGET` bounds skip records only, and the frame terminator's `records=` field is reconciliation metadata rather than a ceiling: it reports what was emitted, it does not cap it.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31 from §36's design review, which rated the gap [high] on the host side and recommended a producer budget as the other half. §36 shipped the host half (streaming reconciliation, input bounds that refuse rather than degrade, artifact caps with explicit truncation fields); this section owns the kernel half. It is deliberately NOT part of §36: the emitter is `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c` + the `sys_write`/`NtWriteFile` capture path, which is §33's SMP-sensitive blast radius, not §36's host scripts.

- [x] Derived a per-owner RECORD budget (`UTEST_CAPTURE_OWNER_RECORD_BUDGET`, 1425) and enforced it in the emitter, so one binary cannot monopolize the serial wire
      - A byte budget was the wrong unit and the derivation is what showed it: a binary calling `write()` one byte at a time pays a FULL ~350-byte klog record per raw byte, so a raw-byte cap would let 64 KiB of payload cost 22 MB of wire. Counting records is what bounds the serial cost.
      - Derived, not picked: `ceil(UTEST_CAPTURE_OWNER_RAW_MAX / UTEST_CAPTURE_CHUNK_MAX)` -- the records it takes to deliver the declared 64 KiB raw allowance at worst-case chunk fill, so a binary that fills every chunk is never clipped and the count is the TIGHT one for that (both directions asserted by unit test, which the `_Static_assert`s cannot express).
      - `UTEST_CAPTURE_WIRE_MAX` is derived fresh rather than reusing `UTEST_RECORD_WIRE_MAX`: capture records are emitted while the launcher's color scope is active, so klog's `test_color` branch (`klog.c:1305-1308`) adds a 19-byte truecolor sequence the skip-marker model does not carry. Reusing it would have proven a ceiling BELOW the traffic actually emitted.
- [x] Emits ONE authenticated `[UTEST-CAPTURE-OVER] owner=N seq=N scope=owner|run limit=N charged=N` marker, then stops capturing for that owner
      - `LOG_WARN`, deliberately distinct from `[UTEST-CAPTURE-LOST]`'s `LOG_ERROR`: a budget stop is expected behavior under an abusive binary, and conflating them would make a policy stop read as corruption.
      - Post-stop bytes are SWALLOWED (`test_usermode_capture_byte` keeps returning 1) rather than handed back to the caller's raw `serial_putchar` fallback -- falling back would put the identical payload on the wire unframed and the budget would bound nothing.
- [x] Taught the host reconciler the marker: a budgeted stop is a BOUNDED payload, kept green, in `scripts/utest-capture.py`
      - Reported through its OWN `budget_stop`/`budget_scope`/`budget_limit` fields (and a separate `capture.budget_stop` XML property), NOT through `truncated`: that flag is paired with an exact `truncated_bytes = total - retained` count, and the producer cannot know how many bytes the binary wrote after it stopped listening. The design review rated folding them together a [medium] false-exact field.
      - The marker suppresses `capture_unterminated`, so it is the one record worth forging and is checked accordingly: it must be the owner's highest seq, leave no sequence hole, and sit at EXACTLY its declared limit when `scope=owner`. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §36 (item: "Reconcile captured-versus-declared bytes FAIL-CLOSED, now that both sides count exactly the same bytes")
- [x] Added a run-wide budget (`UTEST_CAPTURE_RUN_RECORD_BUDGET`, 22795) so N binaries each under the per-owner cap cannot sum past the run ceiling
      - A run-scope stop cannot be proven from one owner's stream (that owner is legitimately far below its own limit), so the host proves it against the run instead: every `scope=run` marker must declare the same limit, and the run must have charged at least that many chunk records across all owners.
      - The budget decision, the sequence draw, the run charge and the owner's stop latch are ONE critical section under `s_capture_budget_lock`; emission stays outside it (klog's serial write is milliseconds). That linearization is what makes the marker always the highest seq and every drawn sequence number map to a record -- the design review rated the lock-free two-counter version [high] for exactly the gaps it leaves.
      - Scoped to the FRAMED RUN, not boot lifetime, and a run generation narrows that binding across the emission gap: a claim charged to a previous run is DROPPED rather than attributed to the current one.
      - It NARROWS rather than closes: the generation is compared under the lock and the record is emitted after releasing it, so a preempted stale emitter can still cross a run boundary. Two review rounds rated that [high]; it is knowingly accepted here because the same race perturbs frame counts and owner reconciliation for record kinds this budget never touches, and the real fix (drain in-flight emitters, or reap the captured descendant tree, before the next frame publishes) is a framing-layer change. Stated at the code too, not just here. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §49 (item: "Reap or fence the whole CAPTURED DESCENDANT TREE at the run boundary, not just the top-level pid")
- [x] Commit: `"test: bound per-binary capture emission at the producer with an authenticated overflow marker"`

**Test checkpoint:** a fixture binary emitting past its per-owner budget produces exactly one authenticated overflow marker, stops emitting capture records, and the run stays green with that binary's artifact payload marked `budget_stop` against the producer's declared scope and limit; the run-wide budget trips on many small emitters rather than only on one large one; a budgeted stop is never reported as `capture_unterminated`, and an owner with no marker still is. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test.sh SUITE=exec` (7 new `TEST_CAT_EXEC` budget regressions; 1506 kernel + 17 user-mode pass) and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` (12 new host reconciler assertions; 1030/1030 pass).

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped: a derived per-owner record budget (1425) and run-wide ceiling (22795) in `test_usermode.c`, one authenticated `[UTEST-CAPTURE-OVER]` terminator per owner, and host acceptance of it in `utest-capture.py` as a bounded stop.
> - Integrates through `s_capture_budget_lock`: the budget verdict, sequence draw, run charge and owner latch are one critical section, so the terminator is always the owner's highest seq and no sequence number is ever drawn without a record.
> - Downstream: `utest-json-harvest.py` gains `captured_budget_stop`/`_scope`/`_limit` and the JUnit XML a `capture.budget_stop` property, kept separate from `truncated` because that field's byte count is exact and a producer stop's is not.
> - Also fixed a latent gap the review surfaced: the emitter drew a sequence number BEFORE escaping, so an escape refusal would have left a permanent hole the host reads as lost output.
> - Scope boundary: publishing the producer's bounds ON the wire (write identity, spawn expectation, chunk bound) is §48's work, so the budgets stay kernel-side declared allowances here.

> Split 2026-07-31: this section was filed carrying eight open items and the section-manifest complexity predictor rated it SPLIT-RECOMMENDED.
