## 42. Ring-3 Entry Evidence and Wall-Clock Assertions in the Reap-Grace Regressions

Two unrelated-looking gaps that share one shape: a claim about a run is decided by a CLOCK rather than by evidence the code actually recorded. §37 left a timeout after frame publication unclassifiable because nothing records ring-3 entry, and the reap-grace regressions decide whether a grace was rejected by measuring elapsed seconds.

The four zero-grace regressions in `scripts/test-tooling.sh` (`a zero grace ('0'|'00'|'.0'|'0.0') falls back to the default, not an instant kill`) assert an ELAPSED WALL-CLOCK bound -- `elapsed >= 4` against the 5-second default -- measured across two independent integer-second truncations: `$SECONDS` in the harness and the deadline arithmetic inside `utest_reap_qemu`. Each can shed most of a second, so a loaded host observes 3 and reds the suite on code that is behaving correctly.

> [!NOTE]
> Observed live 2026-07-31 during §37's fix loop, TWICE and on DIFFERENT assertions: first `.0` with `elapsed=3: state=reaped elapsed=3`, then `utest_reap_qemu force-ends a SIGTERM-resistant VM on a deadline` in a later run -- each passing on the immediately following run with no code change in between. Two distinct members of the same group failing the same way is why the second item below is a sweep rather than a fix to the four zero-grace cases alone. Filed rather than fixed inline because it belongs to §34's QEMU reap work, not to §37's classification work, and a timing-bound test wants a structural replacement rather than a widened bound -- widening `>= 4` to `>= 3` would make the assertion accept the instant kill it exists to detect. A dated hazard line is in `.claude/state/live-gotchas.md` until this ships.

- [ ] Record positive ring-3 ENTRY evidence per child, so a timeout after frame publication stops being ambiguous
      §37 classifies a binary that never ran as `<error>`/`ERROR`, and closes every case it can prove: a loader that EXITED early (marked at each exit) and a timeout with NO frame published. What it cannot close is a timeout AFTER publication -- an adopted frame hanging in ring 3 is indistinguishable from one never adopted, so it conservatively stays a `<failure>`.
      - **`exec_pending` cannot serve as the evidence**: `src/kernel/sched/task.c` force-clears it after `EXEC_PENDING_STUCK_TICKS` (10 ticks) against a 10-second test timeout, so by the time the launcher looks, a never-adopted frame reads exactly like an adopted one.
      - **The fix is scheduler-side**: set a per-task `entered_user` at the point `schedule()` adopts the exec frame (where it clears `exec_pending`), and have the launcher read it at timeout. That is a core-scheduler edit with SMP blast radius, which is why it is filed rather than folded into a test-framework section.
      - Found by §37's round-7 adversarial review -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §37 (item: "Decided: a refusal belongs in JUnit `<error>`, and the accounting was extended to match")
- [ ] Replace the elapsed-seconds bound with a structural invariant that cannot be shed by truncation
      The assertion's real claim is "the zero grace was REJECTED and the default substituted", which the code can state directly -- e.g. have the reap path expose the grace it resolved (or observe the SIGTERM-to-SIGKILL gap from the recorded signal log rather than from `$SECONDS`), so the test reads a decision rather than a duration -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §34 (item: "Commit: `\"test: detect and reap orphaned QEMU processes before a run\"`")
- [ ] Sweep the sibling reap regressions in the same group for the same shape, and state the bound each one actually needs
      The escalation and cancel tests around it read signal LOGS rather than clocks and are sound; the sweep is to confirm no other assertion in the group depends on a wall-clock margin, so the fix does not leave one flaky case behind while claiming the class is closed
- [ ] Bind the loader-stage evidence to the CHILD rather than to two process-wide globals, so it cannot outlive its invocation
      `s_loader_stage_fault` and `s_loader_reached_exec` (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c`) carry no pid or invocation identity: the launcher clears both per spawn and reads them after the wait, and the whole never-ran-versus-failed classification rests on that pairing holding.
      - **It holds today only because of a single-dispatch invariant, not because of the atomics.** The force-kill path states it outright -- task dispatch uses one global `current_task` and APs do not run scheduled tasks -- so a force-killed loader cannot be mid-store on another CPU. The globals' own comment claims the release/acquire pair makes them safe across CPUs, which is a stronger claim than what actually holds.
      - **The day APs dispatch tasks, a late store from a timed-out loader lands after the launcher has already reset the flags for the NEXT binary**, changing that binary's ERROR-versus-FAIL classification -- a misclassification the artifacts would reconcile cleanly, or a harvester refusal attributed to an unrelated prior binary.
      - **Shape**: move both into the child TCB (or another pid-keyed record) and read them from the specific child being reaped; the existing `entered_user` item above wants the same home, so land them together. Add a regression that force-kills a loader and proves the next binary's classification is unaffected.
      - Found by §37's round-10 adversarial review -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §37 (item: "Decided: a refusal belongs in JUnit `<error>`, and the accounting was extended to match")
- [ ] Commit: `"test: assert the reap-grace decision structurally instead of by wall-clock margin"`

**Test checkpoint:** the four zero-grace regressions pass 20 consecutive runs of `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` on a host under parallel build load, and an artificially instant grace (the behaviour they exist to catch) still fails them. Test on: host tooling only (no QEMU dependency).

## 43. Distinguishable Identity Kind for Aggregate Refusals

The five fail-closed aggregates publish through the SAME `refused_<n>_<prefix>_<8hex>.exe` shape as a per-name refusal, but their digest field carries a COUNT rather than `fnv1a32` of the label: `enumeration_plan` passes `UTEST_PLAN_PAGES`, `enumeration_plan_full` passes `plan.count`, `manifest_refusal_overflow` passes `manifest.refused_count`, `glob_refusal_overflow` passes `glob_refusal_count`, and `manifest_unparseable` passes `0` (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c`). No filename produced any of them, so the §37 correlator's whole-identity contract -- reproduce both the digest and the sanitized prefix -- cannot hold for the class.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31 from §37's round-10 adversarial review, rated [medium]. It is NOT §37's scope to fix: the correlator is §37's, but the identity SHAPE these aggregates borrow is §35's enumeration-plan work, and the digest field is carrying the count deliberately (it is the only place the number of lost entries survives). Closing it means either giving the count its own field or giving the aggregate its own record shape -- both change a record whose width feeds the derived name bound, which is why it is a section rather than a §37 follow-up. §37 shipped the honest doc instead: `docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md` now states that aggregates are not correlatable and why.

- [ ] Give aggregate refusals an identity kind a consumer can tell apart STRUCTURALLY, without mirroring a list of kernel labels
      A host-side allowlist of `enumeration_plan`/`manifest_unparseable`/... is the mirrored-constant failure this subsystem has already refused twice (the correlator deliberately does not mirror `UTEST_MAX_BINARY_NAME`; §40 owns the same problem for the chunk bound). The distinction has to be in the SHAPE.
      - **Options to weigh**: a distinct prefix (`aggregate_<n>_<label>_<count>.exe`) that the correlator's regex simply does not match; or keeping the shape and moving the count into its own record field so the digest can go back to being `fnv1a32(label)`.
      - **Either way the derived name bound has to be re-derived, not adjusted by hand** -- the aggregate shape feeds `UTEST_MAX_BINARY_NAME` through the same maximum-over-reachable-shapes rule §37 used for the never-ran record.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §37 (item: "Give an operator a path from a refusal identity back to the offending file. `refused_<n>_<prefix>_<8hex>.exe`")
- [ ] Teach `scripts/utest-refusal-id.py` the new kind: report an aggregate as an aggregate rather than searching for a filename that produced it
      Today `match` on an aggregate identity reports no match, and can in principle name an unrelated candidate that happens to hash to the count -- a false attribution the tool's own all-candidates rule cannot catch, because the digest genuinely matches.
- [ ] Add host and kernel vectors covering every one of the five aggregate paths, so a new aggregate cannot be added with a non-conforming identity
- [ ] Commit: `"test: give aggregate refusals an identity kind the correlator can recognize"`

**Test checkpoint:** each of the five aggregate refusal paths publishes an identity the correlator classifies as an aggregate (naming the label and the count it carries) rather than attempting a filename correlation; a per-name refusal is unaffected and still correlates exactly as §37 shipped it; the derived name bound is recomputed from the reachable shapes and its static assert still holds. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

## 44. Close the Residual Completeness Gaps §37's Review Found

Three findings from §37's review round 10 that are real but out of that section's scope. They share one shape: a guarantee is stated over a population, and the mechanism enforcing it covers only part of that population. §37 fixed the two members of the class that were its own (the stale bound rationale, and a completeness test looping over 6 of 7 record kinds); these three have owners elsewhere or need their own verification cycle.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31. Sources: the Opus `kernel-quality-auditor` walk (whose lead SMP/bare-metal dimension came back CLEAN for §37 -- these are derivation-integrity and artifact-symmetry findings, not races) and the `parity-research-analyst` pass. The parity dispatch could NOT reach the web for the THIRD consecutive time (`websearch_offload_gate.py` R4 blocks the researcher agent it reroutes to), so its JUnit/kselftest/TAEF characterizations are recalled rather than cited; the finding below rests on repo code read at file:line, not on the blocked research.

- [ ] Give the JSON artifact the same self-describing abort signal the XML artifact already has
      On an aborted run `scripts/test.sh` injects a synthetic `<testcase name="suite-abort"><error/></testcase>` (`scripts/test.sh:2508`), so ANY generic JUnit consumer reds the build just by walking testcase elements. `scripts/utest-json-harvest.py` has no equivalent: the abort rides only as `summary.aborted`.
      - **The consequence is the exact false-completeness class this whole subsystem exists to close**: a consumer following the doc's own worked recipe (`jq -c '.testcases[] | {name, status, time_ms}'`) sees a smaller but internally consistent, apparently-green set on an aborted run, and every count reconciles because the binaries that never ran were never published as records.
      - **JSON's stated audience makes it worse, not better** -- the doc names dashboards, trend analysis and regression bisect, which are precisely the generic consumers least likely to special-case a boolean.
      - **Shape**: emit a synthetic `record_kind:"binary"` ERROR record mirroring the XML `suite-abort` one, and reconcile it on both sides like every other record; keep `summary.aborted` as well, since a structured consumer should not have to pattern-match a name.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §37 (item: "Decided: a refusal belongs in JUnit `<error>`, and the accounting was extended to match")
- [ ] Extend the reason-width lint to the five LAUNCHER-COMPOSED reasons, not just the refusal set
      `scripts/utest-reason-lint.py` (§37) binds the `UTEST_RSN_*` refusal literals to their size tree, which is 9 of the 14 reason widths. The other five -- `UTEST_REASON_TIMEOUT`/`EXIT`/`LEAK`/`ISOLATE`/`INVALID` -- are composed from literals hand-duplicated at their call sites (`"timeout after "`/`"ms"`, `"exit="`, `" handle(s) leaked"`, `"isolation failed"`, `"invalid test report"`) with no macro, no `_Static_assert` and no lint tying the two copies together.
      - **Widening any call-site literal leaves `UTEST_REASON_MAX` understating the real width**, which makes the derived `UTEST_MAX_BINARY_NAME` too generous and pushes bound-length names into the overflow fallback the build otherwise proves unreachable.
      - Not memory-unsafe -- `reason[UTEST_REASON_BUF]` is 96 and every append is checked -- so the exposure is a silently wrong derivation, which is the same failure class the lint was written to close.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §37 (item: "Bind the `UTEST_RSN_*` literal set to the `UTEST_REASON_REFUSAL` size tree mechanically. Every reason string must appear")
- [ ] Publish a framed verdict line on the `task_create` failure path, like every sibling refusal does
      That path increments the failed and never-ran counters and emits TAP/XML/JSON records, but logs a plain `klog(LOG_ERROR, "UTEST", "%s: task_create failed", ...)` whose shape does not match the verdict grammar the host recounts. `u_emit_refusal_named` emits the framed line and says why: the host's fail-closed recount counts those very lines against the summary.
      - Not fail-open today -- `scripts/test.sh` escalates only when observed EXCEEDS the summary, and this path makes observed smaller -- so the two counters disagree by construction on exactly the path §37 extended, without tripping anything.
      - Deferred rather than fixed inline because it changes serial output that the host recount parses, which wants its own build + e2e verification rather than a late-round edit to a section already at review round 10.
- [ ] Commit: `"test: close the artifact and derivation completeness gaps from section 37's review"`

**Test checkpoint:** an aborted run's JSON artifact carries a synthetic never-ran ERROR record that a consumer walking `testcases[]` alone reads as red, reconciled on both sides like any other record; the reason lint fails when any of the five composed reason literals is widened without its size tree following; the `task_create` failure path publishes a framed verdict line and the host's fail-closed recount agrees with the summary on that path. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

## 45. Reader Leases for a Resolved Generation

§38 made a leg resolve through one atomically replaced pointer, which closes the mixed-generation READ. It does not close the generation's LIFETIME: the pin `utest_prune_records` grants is a snapshot taken at prune start, so the instant a leg publishes again, the record the previous pointer named loses its protection and a later prune may remove it -- while a consumer that already resolved that pointer is still walking up to its first `open`.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31 from §38's adversarial review, which rated it [high]. §38 answered the read half honestly: `docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md` documents a re-resolve on `ENOENT`, which terminates because the record the CURRENT pointer names is always pinned, and immutability covers everything after the descriptors are held. That is a consumer-side contract, and §38's own text argued a consumer-side contract is the right shape for a window a rename cannot revoke. What it leaves standing is that a well-behaved reader can still be forced to restart through no fault of its own, and a reader that cannot restart (a one-shot pipeline, a CI step reading over a mount) has no answer at all. Deferred out of §38 rather than fixed inline because a lease is a new lifecycle mechanism, not an edit: it needs a grammar, an expiry, a crash-recovery story for a lease whose holder died, and a decision about whether a lease may block retention indefinitely or merely deprioritise it -- an operator-reserved design call, and exactly the class an unattended run files rather than invents.

- [ ] Decide the lease mechanism and its expiry, weighing a lease FILE inside the record against a pointer-side generation hold
      - State what happens when a lease outlives its holder. The bound matters more than the shape: an unbounded lease turns `UTEST_RECORD_KEEP` back into a suggestion, which is the same disk-bound defeat §38's review already caught once in the pin.
- [ ] Implement it so a resolved generation cannot be pruned while a reader holds it, and a dead holder's lease is reclaimed without operator action
- [ ] Add a regression that resolves a pointer, advances the leg, drives the record past the cutoff, prunes, then opens BOTH documents -- the case §38's paused-reader test cannot reach
      - That test's own prune still sees and pins the old target, which is precisely why it cannot expose this window.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §38 (item: "Gave the per-leg alias set a generation pointer, so a concurrent reader cannot pair two runs' documents")
- [ ] Update the resolution algorithm in `docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md`: the `ENOENT` re-resolve becomes the fallback for an UNLEASED reader rather than the whole contract
- [ ] Commit: `"test: hold a resolved run record against retention with a reader lease"`

**Test checkpoint:** a reader that resolves a leg pointer and takes a lease can open and read both documents of that generation after the leg has published again AND retention has run past the cutoff; a lease whose holder is gone is reclaimed by the next prune rather than pinning the record forever; `UTEST_RECORD_KEEP` still bounds the directory in the presence of leases. Test on: host tooling only (no QEMU dependency).

## 46. Inode-Safe Classification in the Pointer Sweep

The retention sweep §38 shipped decides an entry's fate from its pathname: `[ -L ]` or `! [ -f ]` removes it, and anything else is parsed by `sed`. A check and an open are two syscalls, so the classification and the action apply to whatever the NAME resolves to at each moment, not to one verified object.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31 from §38's review round 3, rated [medium]. §38 fixed the CONSEQUENCE portably -- the parse is `timeout`-bounded, so a FIFO swapped in after the type check costs seconds and gets the pointer swept, rather than blocking at startup and wedging every later invocation forever. What it cannot do is make the pair atomic: bash has no `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK` open and no way to compare an inode against the object it already opened. Two residual windows remain, both requiring a writer racing the sweep: a regular file replaced by a special file between check and parse, and a special file replaced by a legitimate new pointer between check and `rm`, which would delete the newcomer. Neither is reachable from a second harness invocation -- `scripts/test.sh` holds an exclusive `flock` and nothing else in the tree writes `build/test-results-*.run` -- so this is a robustness item, not a live defect, and it is filed rather than fixed because the fix needs a non-bash primitive on a path that must still work when python3 is absent (the harness has a documented no-`python3` artifact fallback).

- [ ] Decide the mechanism, weighing a small helper against dropping the sweep's tolerance for non-regular entries entirely
      - A helper must not become a hard `python3` dependency on the startup path; if it would, the honest alternative is to sweep by type without ever parsing, and accept that a hand-made pointer is removed rather than diagnosed.
- [ ] Classify, parse and unlink through ONE verified object, so a replacement between the check and the action cannot be parsed or deleted in place of what was checked
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §38 (item: "Gave the per-leg alias set a generation pointer, so a concurrent reader cannot pair two runs' documents")
- [ ] Add a replacement-race regression that swaps a checked entry between the classification and the action, in both directions
      - The `timeout` bound stays regardless: it is the thing that keeps a lost race cheap, and a regression should prove the sweep still completes when it loses.
- [ ] Commit: `"test: classify and sweep leg pointers through one verified object"`

**Test checkpoint:** a regular pointer replaced by a FIFO between classification and parse leaves the sweep completing and the pointer swept, never blocked; a special entry replaced by a valid new pointer between classification and unlink leaves the NEW pointer in place; the sweep still completes under the `timeout` bound when it loses either race. Test on: host tooling only (no QEMU dependency).

## 47. End-to-End Proof of the Loader's Identity-Mismatch Branch

§39 froze executable identity and verifies it over the exact buffer `task_exec` consumes, and its unit tests prove the mechanism -- the digest is content-bound, the verifier is fail-closed, and an unfreezable entry becomes a counted refusal. What none of them can prove is the INTEGRATION property the §39 test checkpoint actually asks for: that the production `utest_loader_func` hashes the staging buffer, takes the mismatch branch, never reaches `task_exec`, and emits exactly one reconciled record for the planned entry. A helper-only test stays green if that call is moved below `task_exec` or dropped from the loader entirely, which is precisely the regression worth catching.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31 from §39's design review, which rated the gap [medium] and recommended an end-to-end launcher fixture. It is deliberately NOT §39's scope, and not because of effort: staging a sacrificial binary onto the image is a `Makefile` edit, and `receipt_surface_guard.py` BLOCKS the receipt surface (`Makefile*`, the build script, the ABI generator) for the unattended runner. An attended session is the only thing that can land it. Two hazards the design must clear, both established while rejecting the reviewer's original in-place-overwrite fixture: ring 3 has no unlink or rename syscall (`user/include/syscall.h` exposes READFILE/READDIR/OPENFILE/WRITEHANDLE only), so the replacement has to be an in-place write; and `test-smoke-matrix.sh` boots ONE image four times, so a fixture that mutates a binary must stay idempotent across those boots rather than passing on leg 1 and diverging on legs 2-4.

- [ ] Stage a sacrificial planned binary and prove the loader's mismatch branch fires end-to-end, with the payload's side
      effect asserted ABSENT so the test fails if the replacement ever executes -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §39 (item: "Added 3 `TEST_CAT_EXEC` regressions in `src/kernel/test/test_usermode_launcher.c` covering the mechanism over the real VFS" at line 1613)
      - Idempotence across the 4-leg matrix is the constraint that shapes the fixture: a fixed payload makes boot 1 mismatch and boot 2 MATCH (the freeze then digests the already-replaced bytes), silently inverting the test. Deriving each write from the current content instead keeps every boot a genuine mismatch
- [ ] Assert the host side of the same event: exactly one record for the planned entry carrying the named
      `planned binary replaced` reason, `not_run` still reconciling, and a red run on both TCG and KVM
- [ ] Bind the loader's per-spawn state to the spawned task rather than to file-scope globals, covering
      `s_pending_test_path`, the two stage flags and `s_pending_expect_digest` together
      - Raised as [medium] against §39, which took the release/acquire half and pushed back on the rest: `s_pending_expect_digest` carries the identical contract the pre-existing `s_pending_test_path` already had (documented at `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c:27-33`), so binding one of the three in isolation buys no invariant. Not live today -- one boot-time caller, spawn-one-wait-one -- but the launcher is the only thing serialising it
- [ ] Move `UTEST_EXIT_TIMEOUT` out of the `-(signum)` range into the reserved `TASK_EXIT_REASON_BASE` block, and
      migrate the host artifact parsers that key on its current value in the same change
      - Surfaced by section 39's re-adversarial round, which caught the SAME defect in the identity marker it was reviewing: a small negative status is indistinguishable from a signal death, so a binary killed for outrunning its clock and one killed by signal 6 report identically. `include/kernel/sched/task.h:1229-1236` already reserves a block below `-SIG_MAX` and pins it with a `_Static_assert`; the identity marker moved there as `TASK_EXIT_UTEST_IDENTITY`, and `UTEST_EXIT_TIMEOUT` (-6) is now the only one left inside the signal range
      - Deliberately NOT fixed alongside it: the value is load-bearing for the host-side artifact parsers, so the constant and every consumer have to move together
- [ ] Commit: `"test: prove the identity-mismatch branch end-to-end and bind loader state per spawn"`

**Test checkpoint:** a planned binary whose bytes are replaced before its turn produces a named `planned binary replaced` record, its payload's side effect is absent from the run, `not_run` reconciles, and the host fails the run identically on the first and fourth boot of the same image. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

## 48. Wire-Published Facts the Host Enforces: Write Identity, Spawn Expectation, Chunk Bound

Three gaps that share one shape: the host has to certify a property the wire never carries, so it either infers the property (and certifies a truncated or vanished stream) or mirrors a kernel-side constant (and refuses every run the day that constant is re-derived). The fix in each case is the same -- the producer PUBLISHES the fact, and the host enforces what it was told instead of what it guessed.

> [!NOTE]
> Split out of §40 on 2026-07-31, verbatim, when the section-manifest complexity predictor rated the combined eight-item section SPLIT-RECOMMENDED. §40 keeps the producer emission budget (one mechanism: derive, marker, host acceptance, run ceiling); this section keeps the three publish-the-fact items. Both touch `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c` and `scripts/utest-capture.py`, so §40 lands first and this section builds on the wire format it leaves. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §40 (item: "Emit ONE authenticated overflow marker when a budget is exhausted, then stop capturing for that owner")

- [ ] Carry a per-write identifier (or explicit write-start/end records) so every write() call is INDEPENDENTLY terminable, and reconcile each write rather than only the owner
      Today `final=1` terminates one write but the host can only check that the owner's HIGHEST seq is final, so a concurrent writer can mask an earlier unterminated one: with a fork descendant or a second thread sharing the owner, writer A emits `seq=0 final=0` and then faults or is killed before its tail, writer B emits `seq=1 final=1`, and the sequence is contiguous with a final highest record -- so the host certifies an owner whose first write was truncated. No record is missing, so frame-level record counting cannot see it either, and no host-side reconciler can close it because the wire format carries no write identity. Found by the §36 round-2 adversarial review. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §36 (item: "Reconcile captured-versus-declared bytes FAIL-CLOSED, now that both sides count exactly the same bytes")
      - §40's `[UTEST-CAPTURE-OVER]` terminator INHERITS this exposure and slightly widens it, which is an accepted residual owned here rather than a §40 defect. Its adversarial review rated the widening [high] and recommended keeping a budget stop fail-closed until write identity lands; that was REJECTED with evidence, because refusing a budgeted stop is verbatim the `capture_unterminated` false-red §40 exists to close. What the marker relaxes is narrow: without it the highest seq must be `final=1`, so at least the LAST write was known-terminated; with it, the write the budget cut is legitimately open. The masking of an EARLIER writer's truncated write is unchanged and pre-existing -- it is this item. Closing write identity closes both at once, and the host check to add then is that every write below the terminator is complete, not merely that the sequence is contiguous.
- [ ] Mark which testcases were SPAWNED (`capture_expected`) so a vanished capture channel cannot read as success
      The §36 population check runs capture-to-testcase only, because "a testcase with no captured output" is legal for infrastructure rows, skip blocks and binaries that never launched. The consequence is that a producer regression which stops arming capture (or stops emitting `[UTEST-CAPTURE-BEGIN]`) yields an EMPTY model that both artifacts accept as ordinary output-free testcases, and the frame count still reconciles because those records were never produced. Closing it needs the artifact schema to distinguish "spawned, so capture was expected" from "never spawned" -- inferring it host-side would refuse legitimate launch-failure records. Both renderers must then require capture for the expected subset, identically. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §36 (item: "Reconcile captured-versus-declared bytes FAIL-CLOSED, now that both sides count exactly the same bytes")
- [ ] Publish the producer's chunk bound on the wire so the host can enforce it without a mirrored constant
      `UTEST_CAPTURE_CHUNK_MAX` derives to 46 raw bytes from `UTEST_RECORD_LINE_MAX`, `UTEST_CAPTURE_FIXED` and `UTEST_CAPTURE_ESCAPE_EXPANSION`, so the host cannot enforce it today: a hardcoded 46 in `scripts/utest-capture.py` would silently refuse EVERY run the day that derivation changes, which is a worse failure than the over-long chunk it rejects. Carry the bound in the `[UTEST-FRAME]` announcement (or the capture BEGIN record) and have the host reject any declared or decoded chunk above the announced value. Found by the §36 consistency review. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §36 (item: "Reconcile captured-versus-declared bytes FAIL-CLOSED, now that both sides count exactly the same bytes")
- [ ] Commit: `"test: publish write identity, spawn expectation and the chunk bound on the capture wire"`

**Test checkpoint:** a fixture where one write is abandoned mid-stream and a second write from the same owner completes is REFUSED rather than certified; an empty capture model fails a run containing spawned testcases while leaving never-spawned and refusal records green; a record whose decoded chunk exceeds the ANNOUNCED bound is refused, and re-deriving `UTEST_CAPTURE_CHUNK_MAX` changes the announced value without any host edit. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

## 49. Run-Boundary Fence for Emitters That Outlive Their Binary

The launcher waits on the top-level pid only (`u_run_one`), but a `fork()` descendant inherits capture ownership unchanged (`src/kernel/sched/task.c:2459`), so a descendant can outlive the binary it belongs to and keep emitting into whatever run is current when it next reaches klog. `test_usermode_run()` is documented as safe to call repeatedly and `u_frame_begin` reuses the framing nonce, so a stale record is not merely late -- it is AUTHENTICATED and gets counted in a run that never produced it, perturbing `s_frame_records`, the owner/sequence reconciliation, and any run-scoped accounting. Two shipped binaries fork (`user/test/test_process.c`, `user/test/test_faultinject.c`).

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31 from §40's round-2 re-adversarial review, which rated it [medium]. §40 closed the half its own mechanism owns -- a capture claim charged to run N is dropped rather than emitted into run N+1, via a run generation bumped in the budget reset and checked immediately before emission -- and deliberately did NOT widen into this one: the residual predates the budget, affects record kinds the budget never touches, and a real fix is a framing-layer lifetime change (drain in-flight emitters, or reap the whole captured descendant tree, before `u_frame_end`). Doing that inside a budget section would have coupled two unrelated mechanisms. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §40 (item: "Added a run-wide budget (`UTEST_CAPTURE_RUN_RECORD_BUDGET`, 22795) so N binaries each under the per-owner cap cannot sum past the run ceiling")

- [ ] Reap or fence the whole CAPTURED DESCENDANT TREE at the run boundary, not just the top-level pid
      - The two candidate shapes the reviews named: track in-flight emission reservations plus a run generation under one lock and refuse to publish the next frame until prior-generation reservations drain; or terminate and wait for the descendant tree before `u_frame_end()`. The first keeps the no-spinlock-across-serial-I/O rule that shaped §40's design; the second is simpler but needs a way to enumerate the tree.
      - This item SUBSUMES the residual TOCTOU §40 knowingly left open, which two independent review rounds rated [high]: §40's generation check compares under the lock and emits after releasing it, so a stale emitter can pass the check, be preempted, and reach klog after another CPU has run `u_frame_begin`. §40 narrowed the window and said so at `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c` (`u_capture_generation_current`); only a drain protocol or a tree reap closes it, and neither belongs inside a budget. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §40 (item: "Added a run-wide budget (`UTEST_CAPTURE_RUN_RECORD_BUDGET`, 22795) so N binaries each under the per-owner cap cannot sum past the run ceiling")
      - Needs an SMP regression that pauses a claimed emitter across a second `u_frame_begin()`; the kernel test surface cannot spawn threads, so this likely means a fault-injection style hook rather than a unit test.
- [ ] Make a stale record's arrival DIAGNOSABLE rather than merely counted, so the run that inherits it is not the one that fails
      - Today the record is authenticated and indistinguishable from a current-run one, so the failure surfaces as a reconciliation refusal against an innocent run.
- [ ] Regression: a descendant that pauses after its claim and resumes across a second `u_frame_begin()` must not perturb the second run's counts
- [ ] Commit: `"test: fence capture emitters that outlive their binary at the run boundary"`

**Test checkpoint:** two consecutive `test_usermode_run()` invocations with a forked descendant deliberately emitting across the boundary leave the second run's frame count, owner reconciliation and capture budget exactly as if the descendant had not existed; the stale emitter is reported against the run that produced it. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Parity state (§1-§17 shipped):** every 💎 parity row is ✅ except `Perf regression`, which is ⚠️ because §8 asserts against hardcoded thresholds rather than tracking a historical baseline over time -- threshold regressions fail the run, but silent drift below the threshold would not. Closing that to full ✅ needs launcher-side `tests/perf-baseline.json` drift detection, which is deferred to §8 follow-up pending an env-passing syscall (tracked inline in §8's Deferred stamp). **⭐ exclusives:** §5 fault-inject bridge gives a typed `test=1`-gated kernel-allocator probe surface that AppVerifier hooks Win32 for and Linux only exposes through debugfs; §14 Win32-on-non-Win depends on `D02T12 §6` Win32 thunk landing; §17 closes the fast-path stability floor (probes + ABI generator + invariant panic) so TEB/KUSD/syscall transports are as robust as Win11 TEB reads and Linux vDSO calls; §18 will put Impossible OS past both competitors by baking versioned ABI handshake, self-describing KUSD, always-on ring-3 invariants, always-on transition ring-buffer dumps, and in-boot transport fuzzing into the baseline kernel -- capabilities that on Win11 and Linux require opt-in profilers, external fuzzers, or debug-only builds. **❓ = competitor cell UNVERIFIED.** The two §24 artifact rows carry ❓ because the parity research for them could not be sourced: the researcher's `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` were blocked for the whole dispatch, so the TRX and kselftest/TAP claims are recollection, not citation. The Impossible OS column is measured from the shipped artifacts and stands; re-verifying the competitor cells (and deciding whether `utest-json-v1` should align with a published cross-tool schema) is owned by §27. §27 could NOT clear them either: web access was blocked for a second consecutive dispatch, this time with the cause identified (the R4 headless reroute blocks the researcher agent it reroutes TO, so both ends are blocked -- filed against the runner TODO). The two ❓ rows §27 adds carry it for the same reason. §36's `Per-binary captured stdout` row carries ❓ for the SAME web-access reason as §24/§27's -- the Impossible OS cell is measured (17/17 binaries, byte-identical across both artifacts, reconciled fail-closed), and it is marked ⭐ rather than 💎 because neither competitor reconciles captured bytes against a producer-declared count at all: they carry whatever the harness scraped. §37's two rows carry ❓ for the THIRD-CONSECUTIVE-BLOCK reason: its review DID dispatch `parity-research-analyst`, but `websearch_offload_gate.py` rule R4 blocked both `WebSearch` and `WebFetch` for the researcher agent it reroutes TO, so the TRX outcome vocabulary and the TAP claim remain recollection rather than citation -- the Impossible OS cells are measured (984 tooling assertions, both assemblers reconciled fail-closed, `binaries_reported` reconciled against the records carrying a triple) and the competitor cells are what re-verification would change. The analyst's unblocked, code-read findings were acted on: they are §44's JSON-abort-record item and the reason-lint item. §34's `Orphaned-VM recovery` row carries ❓ on a DIFFERENT ground: HLK and kselftest do not launch the machine their tests run on, so there is no published orphan-reaping contract to compare against rather than a claim that went unsourced -- calling that ❌ would assert an absence in a harness the question does not apply to. Everything the ❓ cells would change is a competitor claim, never an Impossible OS one.

The §5 `SYS_FAULT_INJECT` test-mode gate negative regression is the one kernel-side `TEST_CAT_EXEC` assertion that DOES belong in `src/kernel/test/test_syscall.c`.

Result: `test_fastpath.exe FAIL (exit=4)` (probe 3: `gs:0x40 ClientId.UniqueProcess` did not match `sys_getpid`) + `test_fastpath_fuzz.exe FAIL (exit=1)` (`sys_getpid baseline is positive` assertion fired) + wrapper exit=1 + `FAIL: 0 kernel + 2 user-mode of 2200 failed`. Reverted in the same session; subsequent run returned `exit=0` + `PASS: 2184 kernel + 16 user-mode tests passed`.
